中東緊張暫緩，風險資產全面反攻
2026 年 5 月
4 月全球主要股市報酬全面轉正，與 3 月跌勢形成鮮明對比，AI 題材再度成為主軸，重燃市場熱
度，風險資產動能強勁回溫。以美元計價 4 月表現觀察，新興亞洲與新興市場上漲分別上漲 17.4%
與 14.5%，表現最為亮麗，主要受台灣與南韓在 AI 半導體供應鏈帶動下的強勁買盤支撐；標普 500
的 4 月漲幅也創 2020 年 11 月以來最佳月表現 。 然而 ， 拉丁美洲與東協市因與科技題材連結度較低，
使其期間漲幅相對緩和。不過，MSCI 拉丁美洲指數今年前 4 個月的漲幅仍高達 17.3%，為所有主要

股市之冠。
隨著市場風險接受度提高，全球主要債市4 月也同步反彈。新興當地債券單月上漲2.79%表現最
佳，主要因美元指數當月由約 100 降至近 98，使新興當地債換算回美元後的價格水漲船高。波動較
大的亞洲高收益債、新興美元債券、全球高收益債表現也有所表現。全球投資級公司債、全球主權債
券等債券雖亦分別上漲 1.36%、1.25%，但幅度上相對落後。
4 月市場大幅反彈的核心驅動力來自三方面：其一，投資人對中東戰事可能導致通膨上揚的疑慮
明顯鈍化；其二，AI 主軸再度成為主導行情的關鍵主題，包括 Alphabet 雲端與 AI 業務需求暢旺帶
動半導體類股大漲；其三，總經基本面韌性意外優於預期，整體勞動市場仍穩定。然而，霍爾木茲海
峽運輸尚未完全恢復，中東戰事變數仍存，地緣風險仍是市場最大不確定性來源。
根據彭博 5/4 的統計，今年 Q1 與 Q2 EPS 年增率均可望突破兩成，我們預期 2026 年標普 500
企業獲利增長動能持續 ，AI 仍是核心驅動主軸。儘管短期股市可能因中東戰事帶來通膨隱憂而波動，

但我們認為企業基本面強度足以抵銷中東衝突與政策不確定性 ，而企業強勁的獲利成長將可望為全球
股市中期走勢帶來支撐。
債市方面，受到美伊戰爭影響，4 月的 FOMC 會議會後聲明偏鷹，壓抑市場降息預期，考量後續
美伊和談時程仍有不確定性 ，預期長天期公債殖利率波動風險將加劇 。 由於目前美國中短天期公債殖
利率顯著高於過去 10 年均值，且中短期債券到期期間較短，具備資金靈活運用彈性，因此建議在債
券天期選擇上 ， 以中短天期美國公債及高評級投資級債為主 ， 並以平衡投資組合波動角度做適當配置 。

美國股市：美股盈餘增長強勁，AI 主題重獲市場青睞
4 月美股雖受伊朗戰爭不確定性干擾，在財報優異及 AI 產業高速成長帶動下，標普 500 指數續
創歷史新高。指數上揚主要由輝達、Alphabet、亞馬遜、博通、蘋果、微軟、超微、英特爾、美光
等科技股帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括埃克森美孚、雪佛龍、嬌生、默克藥廠、亞培、ServiceNow、
AT&T、洛克希德馬丁、雷神等，市場焦點已經從能源、國防回歸 AI 主題。
美國通膨墊高，但經濟表現仍具韌性，支持聯準會更長時間維持利率。4 月 ISM 製造業 PMI 持
平於 52.7，連續第四個月維持擴張區間。其中供應商交貨指數從 58.9 升至 60.6，反映伊朗戰爭與航

運通道風險升高，導致供應鏈壓力再度升溫；客戶庫存指數降至 39.1，持續偏低；新訂單指數小幅
上升至 54.1，訂單動能尚具支撐，積壓訂單由 54.4 大幅降至 51.4，新出口訂單進一步下滑至 47.9，
顯示外需偏弱；價格指數從 78.3 攀升至 84.6；就業指數降至 46.4，顯示企業尚未恢復積極擴編意願。
故製造業雖維持擴張 ， 但由於庫存已經偏低 ，若戰事持續恐將加速推升通膨。4 月份 ISM 服務業 PMI
指數由前月的 54.0 下降至 53.6，低於市場預期的 53.7，新訂單指數由前月的 60.6 下降至 53.5，低
於市場預期的 57.3，支付價格指數持平於 70.7，物價雖仍在高檔但並未惡化。同時預期新任聯準會
主席 Warsh 上台後，要等到現任主席鮑威爾完全辭去 Fed 理事職務，才會有較大的空間與明確度去
推進「相信 AI 將帶來生產力提振、進而支持降息」的政策論述，故降息時間或許延後，未來利率將
更長時間維持不變。
AI 帶動科技股財報表現優異。預估第一季標普 500 企業盈餘年增可達到 23.9%，且第二季盈餘

增長仍維持近兩成水準，科技板塊受惠於 AI，仍是帶動美股企業獲利增長主軸，但到了第二季，能
源價格維持高檔下將推升能源產業獲利，且若伊朗戰事無法落幕，預料各種原物料價格都將受影響走
高，標普 500 企業獲利成長性並不看淡。
美國金融股第一季財報普遍優於預期，對消費惡化與私募基金流動性的擔憂已經淡化。由於股票
與商品市場交投熱絡，帶動承銷與交易收入顯著成長，各大銀行放款規模持續擴張，淨利息收入成長
優於預期。消費信用方面，信用卡支出與消費貸款餘額仍具韌性，整體資產品質良好。
目前標普 500 在指數續創新高下本益比來到 26 倍、股價淨值比 5.36 倍，短期評價偏高，且通
膨風險逐漸增加、伊朗戰爭的不確定性仍在，雖美國科技龍頭在 AI 產業具備領先優勢，但短期需留
意市場過熱及估值偏高風險，建議以長線角度分批布局。

歐洲股市：歐洲央行態度謹慎，川普加徵歐洲汽車關稅
4 月歐股受能源價格大漲衝擊，雖財報優異但科技股比重相對美股較低，泛歐 600 指數緩步墊高。
指數上揚主要由艾司摩爾、西門子、西門子能源、匯豐、艾波比、英飛凌、施耐德電機、諾基亞、瑞
銀集團、諾和諾德等企業帶動，表現相對弱勢的族群包括殼牌、德意志電信、阿斯特捷利康、諾華、
葛蘭素史克、聯合利華、依視路羅薩奧蒂卡、赫力昂、挪威國家石油、BP 等，市場焦點已經從能源、
國防題材回歸科技與金融股。
4 月歐元區 服務業受到物價飆漲與能源支出暴增的雙重打擊， 服務 業採購經理人指數 急挫至

47.4，創疫情後最差表現；然而製造業採購經理人指數則在新訂單改善(尤其法國)及避險備貨需求的
支撐下，逆勢攀升至 52.2，然而就業人數持續萎縮，反映企業在高通膨與需求不確定性下普遍採取
縮編策略，經濟放緩格局明顯。法國製造業 PMI 攀升至 52.8，主要得益於新訂單走高，反映需求復
甦；德國製造業 PMI 雖達 51.4，增長動能依賴企業拉升庫存，能否延續仍須觀察。
儘管歐洲央行在 4 月利率決策會議按兵不動，但此舉是反映需要更多時間凝聚升息共識，並蒐集
能源衝擊對通膨的實際影響數據 。歐洲央行總裁透露委員會內部曾認真討論升息的可能性，維持不動
的理由包括：近期數據仍接近3 月預測、貨幣政策在能源價格上漲前已處於適當位置、金融與信貸條
件已開始收緊，目前局勢「偏離」原先基準情境，通膨上行風險與經濟成長下行風險均在加劇，但歐
洲經濟尚未進入停滯。市場預期 ECB 將於 2026 年升息 2 碼，甚至不排除於年底或於明年升息第 3
碼，但考量歐洲通膨仍與伊朗戰事息息相關，若能源價格回落、需求走弱，則升息時點可能延後；若

能源價格繼續在高檔盤旋、通膨持續性跡象提早浮現，不排除6 月即會啟動第一次升息。英格蘭銀行
(英國央行)在最新利率決策會議中維持利率不變，但整體立場偏向謹慎，市場同樣預期未來半年可能
有 2 碼升息幅度。近期 ECB 與 BOE 的升息預期已帶動歐元、英鎊轉強。
川普於 5/1 宣布，由於歐盟不遵守貿易協議，將對歐盟進口汽車與卡車加徵 25%關稅(不包含英
國)。由於歐洲議會在 3/26 已有條件通過歐美貿易協議的相關立法，故川普調整汽車業關稅從 15%
到 25%，可能引發美歐貿易戰再次啟動，接下來預料歐盟祭出關稅反制措施且展開新一輪談判。
泛歐 600 指數目前本益比為 16.87，股價淨值比為 2.39，目前面臨能源價格仍在高檔、川普對歐
盟汽車業加徵關稅，短期風險增加。然而考量評價合理、伊朗戰爭持續降溫且預料美歐經過談判，終
將能達成貿易協議，歐股仍可以長線角度、作為分批佈局標的。

日本股市：日本利率按兵不動，AI 題材帶動指數創高
4 月上旬日股受地緣政治風險衝擊，所幸在 AI 題材帶動下科技股走高，日經指數更於 4 月一度
收盤站上 60,537 點，以收盤價站上 6 萬點歷史新高。市場主要由軟銀、愛德萬測試、Keyence、村
田製作所、三菱電機、三菱 UFJ 金融、東京威力科創、藤倉、鎧俠、三井住友金融等公司帶動，表
現相對弱勢的族群包括三菱商事、豐田汽車、任天堂、三井物產、永旺、武田藥品、國際石油開發帝
石、7&I、山之內製藥、東方樂園等，市場焦點回歸 AI、國防，以及升息可能帶動的金融股。

相較於歐洲汽車出口至美國關稅再度被川普調高，目前並未傳出美國可能調高日本進口汽車關稅
的訊息，顯示高市早苗先前與川普會晤帶來實質外交成效。日本進入黃金週，但高市首相選擇飛往越
南與澳洲「拼外交」，尤其將澳洲定位為「準同盟」 ，推動以「最上級」護衛艦為基礎的共同開發與輸
出，顯然高市對於國防自主有堅定決心。
日本央行 4 月利率決策會議以 6 比 3 同意將利率維持於 0.75%，三位委員認為應將政策利率調
高至 1.0%，市場對後續升息的預期明顯升溫。日本央行同時將 FY2026 核心 CPI 預測大幅上調 0.9
個百分點至+2.8%，FY2027 上調 0.3 個百分點至+2.3%，這些預測建立在油價將從每桶約 105 美元
回落至 70～80 美元的假設之上 ， 故若油價長期維持高位 ， 通膨前景可能在7 月展望報告中再度上修 ，
而對 GDP 增長預期也小幅下修。對比會後聲明透露出來的鷹派立場，總裁植田和男會後記者會發言
立場則略顯保守，持續淡化央行升息時點，強調需要時間觀察經濟及通膨發展，且提及達成央行經濟

預估的前景下降 ， 尚無法確定需要多久來評估下次升息時點 。 鑒於過於弱勢的日圓匯率可能更進一步
推高國內通膨，日本央行仍有高機率於第三季上調政策利率，若油價再度上行，甚至不排除在 6 月就
採取行動。
截至 4 月 24 日當周，外資買超日股 8,079 億日圓，2026 年以來外資對日股的累計買超金額來
到 7.97 兆日圓，改寫今年以來累計加碼金額紀錄。近期日財長與外匯最高官員相繼釋出干預匯市警
告、加上外資持續匯入且市場對 BOJ 升息的預期，日圓貶值趨勢已扭轉。近期日圓一度單日升值 3%，
顯示 160 為日本政府當局匯價的干預底線，預料近期日圓仍將於 155 至 158 附近震盪。
2026 年以來日股 TOPIX 指數受惠於 AI 題材、高市擴大財政刺激、強化軍事安全，但本益比仍
在 19.39 倍，股價淨值比在 1.77 倍。近期日圓守住 160 關卡，匯率穩定可降低投資者不確定性，預
料日本企業股票回購仍持續推進，可望為日股在全球金融市場震盪下帶來支撐。

台灣股市：強勁經濟與產業基本面支撐下的長多格局
4 月台灣股市在 AI 超級週期與全球資金的簇擁下 ， 正式跨越了四萬點 ， 本次台股的突破並非單純
的資金行情，而是具備了極為堅實的總體經濟、強健的產業鏈獲利基本面，以及法規鬆綁帶來的籌碼
結構重塑。
台灣今年初展現了令全球市場矚目的經濟爆發力。主計總處公布的第一季 GDP 成長率概估高達
13.69%，創下近 39 年來的單季最高紀錄。這股強大動能的源頭，清晰地反映在經濟部的三月份外銷
訂單數據上， 三月單月接單金額飆升至 911.2 億美元 ， 年增率高達65.9%， 連續14 個月維持正成長。

資訊通信與電子產品年增率分別高達 120.9%與 73.7%，這顯示台灣在全球 AI 基礎設施、高效能運
算（HPC）以及雲端資料中心建設中，扮演著不可替代的樞紐角色。亮眼的總經數據，為台股的高檔
估值提供了最強而有力的下檔支撐。
中經院 4 月製造業 PMI 大幅跳升 4.9 個百分點至 60.3%，連續 7 個月呈現擴張，更是近 55 個月
以來最快的擴張速度。除了 AI 與半導體的實質需求外，中東地緣政治的緊張局勢促使製造業啟動了
「防禦性提前拉貨」 。短期內這將維持製造業的熱絡，但也必須持續監控輸入性通膨的潛在風險。
宏觀數據之外，台積電於 4 月中旬召開的法說會，無疑是穩住外資與台股軍心的定海神針。財報
顯示，第一季 EPS 達 22.08 元，毛利率以 66.2%飛越財測高標。其中高效能運算（HPC）相關營收
佔比已正式突破六成 （達 61%） ，並上修了2026 全年美元營收成長目標至 30% 以上。這場法說會
不僅確認了台積電高階製程的護城河，也確立了全球 AI 伺服器供應鏈的長線能見度。

在基本面一片大好之際，金管會放寬了主動型基金與主動式 ETF 對單一個股 10%的持股上限，
從 10%放寬至 25%。這項法規鬆綁產生了極為強大的「流動性溢價」效應。過去受限於10%緊箍咒
的機構、 基金資金 ， 如今得以大舉回補台積電 、 聯發科等核心權值股 。這讓大盤指數在衝破四萬點後，
資金高度向權值龍頭集中，成為加權指數的進一步推收力量。
綜合亮眼的總經數據、良好的企業基本面，以及籌碼向大型股集中的法規紅利，我們對台股整體
的長線投資建議維持加碼。然而，目前加權指數與季線的正乖離率已來到 18%的歷史相對高位，意
味著市場隨時可能出現技術性拉回與獲利了結的賣壓。必須強調的是，牛市中的急跌與拉回屬於 「健
康的籌碼換手」 ，並非長線趨勢的反轉。面對當前乖離頗大、但長線看多的盤勢，建議投資人不追高，
待拉回後布局，預留部分現金，保留流動性以迎接拉回時的絕佳買點。

科技類股：半導體產業持續看好，分批佈局雲端巨頭
回顧 4 月份，在地緣政治影響逐漸淡化之後，華爾街對 AI 硬體的「錯失恐懼症（FOMO） 」全面
爆發。費城半導體指數上演了史無前例的連續 18 個交易日上漲，單月狂飆近 40%。這波資金浪潮不
僅將指數推向歷史新高。支撐這波半導體狂飆的是極其扎實的財報利多，不僅英特爾靠著晶圓代工與
CPU 獲利超預期迎來大復活，記憶體族群更步入了史無前例的「超級循環」 。由於AI 伺服器對 HBM
與高階 SSD 需求爆發，導致記憶體陷入結構性大缺貨。美光（Micron）與威騰（WDC）等大廠握

有絕對的定價權，這再次確認了半導體作為「AI 算力基礎設施」的長線多頭地位。
在剛公布的財報中，四大雲端巨頭交出了令人驚豔的成績單。微軟的 AI 相關年度經常性收入大
增，Google Cloud 也寫下逾 60%的驚人成長，Meta 則透過 AI 優化廣告投放，拉升利潤率，亞馬
遜（AWS）的營收增速也創下近期新高。這些數據證明，掌握龐大生態系的巨頭，已經能將 AI 算力
成功轉化為自身業務的現金流。此外，根據 2026 Q1 財報數據，四大雲端服務供應商並未因高基期
而放慢腳步，反而持續加大投入，全年資本支出（Capex）預計由原先估計的 6,000 億美元進一步增
加至近 7,000 億美元。在這波驚人的支出中除了晶片設計商外，代工的台積電、供應HBM 的三星與
海力士，以及伺服器組裝與散熱解決方案的廠商都因此受惠，這也是硬體續漲的主要原因。
然而儘管 CSP 業者的支出續增，但投資人顯然不再全面買單，市場進入「AI 變現檢驗期」 。由於
龐大的折舊壓力與變現的陣痛 ，四大 CSP 業者在財報後的股價表現各不相同 。Google 與亞馬遜成功

用現有核心業務的強勁現金流護航 AI 支出，成為資金的避風港；反觀微軟與 Meta，因資本支出預
期大幅暴增 ， 市場擔憂長期支出深不見底 ，變現遙遙無期 ，積極的佈局反而成為短期壓抑股價的包袱 。
然而四大 CSP 的 EPS 年增率依然維持在雙位數，但評價面卻因資本開支焦慮被壓低，下檔風險相對
受控，這提供了一個逢低布局的機會。
AI 產業的長多格局不變，但在進入 5 月後，市場可能面臨傳統的「Sell in M ay」季節性逆風，
且 2026 年適逢美國期中選舉，歷史經驗顯示第二、三季往往是波動最劇烈的時期。因此，我們建議
半導體類股仍是不可或缺的持股，但也應該開始逢低布局美股七巨頭等大型科技類股。 七巨頭的預估
本益比已經回落至合理區間，且具備穩健的現金流。加上隨著 Google 的 TPU、亞馬遜的 Trainium
等自研晶片部署比例提升，CSP 業者將逐步降低對高價 GPU 的依賴，這將顯著降低長期推論成本。
因此建議適時布局相關類股，來迎接下半年變現能力轉強後的估值修復行情。

AI 電力類股：資料中心用電需求倍增，有利電力基礎建設發展
IEA(國際能源署)發布 2026 年 Key Questions on Energy and AI 報告，指出受到 AI 快速發展影
響，全球用電需求將急遽成長，有利於相關電力基礎建設發展。IEA 指出，全球資料中心用電量在 2024
至 2025 年間成長逾 15%，年增約 70 太瓦時至 485 太瓦時，占全球電力需求略超過 1.5%，未來幾
年用電需求更將進一步加速，IEA 預估全球資料中心用電將從 2024 年的 415 太瓦時倍增至 2030 年
的 950 太瓦時，屆時將占全球電力需求的 3%，AI 專用資料中心用電量更將成長三倍至 465 太瓦時。

驅動電力需求暴增的核心因素是 AI 伺服器功耗密度的指數級攀升。以 Nvidia 為例，2020 年
Ampere 架構機架功耗密度約 13 千瓦時，當前 Blackwell 架構已達 130 千瓦時，下一代 Rubin 架構
將躍升至 600 千瓦時， 屆時單一機架尖峰用電量將等同約 65 戶家庭 。在 AI 伺服器功耗上升的同時，
大型科技巨頭資本支出也持續攀升，為 AI 伺服器帶來更多需求，2025 年科技巨頭資本支出合計已超
過 4,000 億美元，2026 年預計再增 75%至 7,150 億美元，IEA 更進一步預估 2026 至 2030 年間資
料中心累計投資將達 3.9 兆美元，隨資料中心大量建置，AI 用電需求將隨之攀升。
此外，IEA 指出儘管單一 AI 任務的能耗效率正以前所未見的速度改善，單一任務功耗每年約可
降至十分之一，然而新興高耗能應用如影片生成、推理及代理式 AI 也持續推陳出新，這些新功能的
能耗可達到簡單文字生成的數百至數千倍 ，導致即便 AI 效率持續提升，整體能耗需求仍然有增無減。

在 AI 用電需求大增的同時，目前電力基礎建設供給端各環節均出現嚴重瓶頸，相關關鍵零組件
因產能不足，導致交期持續延長，包含變壓器交期平均需二至三年，燃氣輪機交期長達五年，電網併
聯在美國多數地區也須排隊長達五至十年。以德州 ERCOT 獨立電網為例，資料中心併網排隊量從
2024 年 12 月的 63 吉瓦時暴增至 2026 年 1 月逾 230 吉瓦時，但該州歷史尖峰負載僅 85 吉瓦時，
顯示電力需求遠超既有電網承載量。可預見隨電力需求持續攀升，相關電力基礎建設上中下游未來訂
單積壓量都可望保持高水位，並隨公司產能陸續開出，逐步轉換為公司營收貢獻。
展望後市，IEA 預估全球資料中心發電量至 2030 年將倍增至超過 1,000 太瓦時，其中再生能源
與天然氣各占約三分之一，分別達 360 及 340 太瓦時，核能預料將從 75 太瓦時成長至 120 太瓦時，
故各類相關零組件包含燃氣輪機、變壓器、核能及儲能等子產業都可望受惠這一 AI 用電趨勢，隨 AI
電力需求持續上升，對於各類電力上中下游供應鏈都可正向看待。

能源類股：海峽受阻導致油價居高不下；阿聯酋退出 OPEC 有利戰後供給恢復
西德州原油期貨價格 4 月再度攀升至每桶 100 美元之上，主因經荷莫茲海峽的石油出口持續受
阻，加上美伊談判持續未見明顯進展，同時期間內美國總統川普已數次拒絕伊朗所提出之協議，美國
對伊朗的封鎖行動也持續進行，故在全球石油供需缺口維持的狀況下，全球石油庫存水位不斷下降，
為石油價格帶來上行壓力。
國際能源署 4 月月報指出，受荷莫茲海峽供應中斷影響，3 月全球石油供應暴跌 1,010 萬桶/日
至每日 9,700 萬桶，其中 OPEC+產量月減 940 萬桶/日至每日 4,240 萬桶，非 OPEC+供應月減 77

萬桶/日至每日 5,470 萬桶，儘管沙烏地西岸、阿聯酋富查伊拉港及伊拉克經土耳其傑伊漢的 ITP 管
線替代路線出口量，已從戰前每日不到 400 萬桶提升至每日 720 萬桶，然而仍無法彌補荷莫茲海峽
的供給中斷，使得中東海灣國家石油出口損失合計仍高達每日 1,300 萬桶，自開戰以來，3 月石油供
給損失已超過 3.6 億桶，國際能源署更進一步預估 4 月石油供給損失將擴大至 4.4 億桶。
在供給大幅下降的同時，國際能源署也同步下修全球石油需求預估，預計 2026 年全年需求將年
減 8 萬桶/日，較上月報告下修 73 萬桶/日，其中 3 月全球需求預計將年減 80 萬桶/日，4 月進一步
擴大至年減 230 萬桶/日，合計第二季需求預估年減 150 萬桶/日，為新冠疫情以來最大單季跌幅，
下調需求預估主因荷莫茲海峽供給中斷，導致眾多仰賴中東石油進口的亞洲塑化廠面臨斷供，在缺乏
原料的狀況下被迫降低需求。
然而儘管目前荷莫茲海峽持續受阻，導致油價居高不下，然而戰後有利油價快速下行的因子也持

續在萌芽。阿聯酋 4/28 宣布退出 OPEC 及 OPEC+，為該組織近年來最重要的成員流失。阿聯酋目
前石油產能為 485 萬桶/日 ， 然而其受限於OPEC 配額 ， 實際產量僅約360 萬桶/日 ， 兩者差距達125
萬桶/日，過去幾年來阿聯酋持續因產量受限問題，而與其他成員國出現爭執，隨阿聯酋退出 OPEC，
將有利於其提升產量上限。雖然目前因荷莫茲海峽仍處封鎖狀態，阿聯酋石油出口同樣受阻，然而一
旦戰後海峽重開，阿聯酋將可不再受 OPEC 配額約束，立即釋放約 125 桶/日的增量供給，阿聯酋國
有石油公司 ADNOC 更規劃將在 2027 年將石油產量上升至 500 萬桶/日、2029 年再進一步上升至
600 萬桶/日。此外阿聯酋退出 OPEC 亦可能引發其他成員跟進 ，使得戰後油價下行風險將顯著增加。
展望後市，當前油價雖受到海峽封鎖影響，中短線上有望持續維持在 100 美元左右震盪，然而
美伊戰事未見進一步擴大，後續油價也難以再創新高，此外若未來美伊談判出現進展，海峽運量出現

緩解，阿聯酋增產也有利戰後原油供需恢復，故我們目前對於能源類股後市中性看待。

黃金類股：全球央行續買黃金，惟短線金價仍受美伊戰爭不利影響
黃金價格 4 月持續在區間內震盪，黃金類股表現也相對弱勢，主因受到美伊戰爭影響，油價上漲
帶動通膨預期攀升，使得市場對於聯準會降息預期下滑，帶動配置黃金的機會成本上升，同時避險
情緒導致美元走強，也不利於黃金等以美元計價的商品資產表現。此外，受到戰爭影響，土耳其動
用黃金儲備以支應外匯與流動性需求，短線拋售黃金也導致金價承壓。
世界黃金協會數據顯示，2026 年 3 月各國央行申報合計淨賣出 30 公噸黃金，主要就是受到土
耳其拋售 60 公噸所拖累，受此影響，合計第一季全球央行申報黃金淨購買量僅有 2 頓。然而若看加

計未申報買盤量，則是完全不同的狀況，根據世界黃金協會調查顯示，2026 年第一季全球央行黃金
購買量加計未通報買盤後，合計淨購買 244 噸，為自 2024 年第四季以來的新高，同時顯著高於過
去五年平均購買水準的 228 噸，顯示檯面下的世界央行購金趨勢仍未改變。
另一方面，在高盛 4 月 29 日發布的最新報告中，維持了對於 2026 年底黃金目標價 5,400 美
元/盎司的預測。高盛指出，目前金價主要驅動因素包括全球央行持續推進外匯儲備多元化、目前相
對偏低的投機性部位回歸常態化，以及聯準會預計在年內降息 50 個基點等三大支撐。在央行需求方
面，高盛指出，在其央行會議調查中，約 70%的受訪央行預期全球黃金儲備將繼續上升。在風險評
估方面，高盛認為短線風險偏向下行，主要因荷莫茲海峽封鎖若持續 ，可能觸發債市或股市修正，
進而引發黃金部位遭到清算， 然而中長期風險則偏向上行，因 伊朗事件與更廣泛的地緣政治發展 ，
如格陵蘭及委內瑞拉議題，可能加速資金轉向黃金配置。
另一方面，來自穩定幣發行商 Tether 的黃金買盤也值得關注。截至 2026 年 3 月底，Tether 為

支撐 USDT 儲備而持有的黃金市值上升至 198 億美元，約為 132 公噸，較 2025 年 12 月底的 126
公噸再增加約 6 公噸 ，此外 Tether 另持有 22 公噸黃金以支撐其黃金代幣 XAUT， 兩項產品合計持倉
達約 154 公噸 ， 此規模已足以排進全球前20 名黃金持有者 。JPMorgan 貴金屬研究主管 Greg Shearer
指出，Tether 的購金行為已對金價產生實質性影響，其 2025 年全年購買量僅次於波蘭央行，並超越
所有其他央行，顯示加密貨幣機構儲備已成為黃金新一個重要買盤來源
展望後市，中長線而言央行增持黃金的結構性趨勢未變，美伊衝突加上格陵蘭、委內瑞拉等地緣
政治風險，也都有望加速各國對黃金的分散配置需求 ，使得黃金有望獲得更多來自央行買盤的支撐。
然而短線上，在美伊戰爭尚未底定前，黃金機會成本上升及部分央行拋售壓力仍將壓抑短線走勢，
故預估黃金將在區間內震盪，目前對於黃金類股後市抱持中性看法。

### Page 10

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
固定收益：華許入主 Fed 機率高，美債波動料加大
FOMC 在 4/29 的會議再度將聯邦基金利率目標區間維持在 3.50-3.75%。然而，理事 Miran 主
張降息一碼，而會後聲明中「傾於寬鬆」(easing bias)的措辭也遭三位地區主席(Cleveland 的
Hammack、Minneapolis 的 Kashkari 與 Dallas 的 Logan )反對 ，會議表決出現 4 票異議 ，創 FOMC
逾 30 年來委員立場最分歧的局面。前述三位地區聯儲主席反對寬鬆的措辭，導致市場對 Fed 後續降
息預期顯著弱化。

此外，川普提名的下任 Fed 主席 Kevin Warsh(華許)於 4/29 參議院銀行委員會審查通過，預計
於 5/11 當週進行全院表決。目前參議院共和黨、民主黨的議員比例為 53：47(含兩名傾向民主黨的
無黨籍議員)，由於除了 Tillis 以外的共和黨參議員均未公開就華許的資格表達反對，未來在參院全院
的表決中，華許也可望取得過半票數通過該任命案。若時程安排得宜，料可於 5/15 鮑爾主席任滿後
接掌 Fed。值得留意的是，鮑爾在 4 月的記者會表示將繼續留任理事會，這將導致未來的 FOMC 將
出現新舊主席並存的罕見現象。
綜觀華許的主要貨幣政策主張為「降息」 +「縮表」 ，兩者在貨幣政策方向呈現相反的效果，且
對不同天期的債券影響亦有差異： 「降息」對於雖使債券殖利率下修，但對短端影響較大；而「縮表」
量化緊縮(QT)雖屬貨幣緊縮政策，但堪薩斯聯儲研究(Smith & Valcarcel 2021)顯示，「縮表」推升期
限溢酬的幅度大於短期利率。兩者同時施行可能造成導致未來美債殖利率曲線淨陡峭化。話雖如此，

美國貨幣政策需要 FOMC 票委以多數決通過，前主席 Yellen 認為華許即便就任 Fed 主席，在表決中
仍難以取得足以支持其貨幣政策路線的票數。因此，即便 Warsh 上任，其政策主張(尤其在縮表的議
題上)短期將無法付緒實現，故預期在可見的未來對美國的貨幣政策走向改變有限。
然而，Fed 主席的權力在於議程設定與對外溝通，華許在聽證會上所表達的部分想法-包括降低
FOMC 會議與記者會舉辦的頻率等，將使 FOMC 決策透明度降低，或導致美國債市波動加大。其表
達改變目前聯準會青睞的通膨指標-核心 PCE 的意圖，也可能使金融市場的不確定性增加。
預期短線債市在通膨疑慮與政策不確定性的交錯下 ， 將持續面臨殖利率高檔震盪的壓力， 但考量
美伊全面開戰可能性極低，油價的通膨疑慮料隨時間鈍化，而 Warsh 立場傾向支持降息，因此就中
期來看，美債殖利率應有走降空間。同時，我們認為中短天期債相較於長天期債券殖利率相去不遠，
投資人無需承擔較高的存續期間更可享有不錯的債息收益 ， 且中短天期到期期間較短亦有資金靈活運

用的優勢，因此建議可以中短天期美國公債/投資級債為主。

数据点评  
进出口双双超预期，出口增势多点发散  
——2026 年 9 月贸易数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院  
[Table_Summary]  摘要：从贸易总体情况看，9 月我国货物贸易出口、 进口同比分别增长36.3%、
31.0%，较上月分别加快 11.3、2.8 个百分点；1-9 月进出口总额同比增长
23.8%，贸易顺差达 9381.1亿美元。展望后续，全球AI 算力投资、汽车及机
电产品出口有望继续提供支撑， 但高基数、海外需求波动及贸易政策不确定性
可能带来扰动。从贸易伙伴看，9 月我国对东盟、韩国、欧盟出口同比分别增
长 52.3%、68.2%、25.3%，较上月分别加快22.1、18.9、18.6个百分点，反
映区域产业链贸易联系深化及 AI 产业链需求增长；对欧出口提速也可能受到
企业提前出货影响。对美贸易增速同步回升， 中美关税减让共识有望改善双边
贸易预期，但具体安排仍待落地。从出口结构看，机电产品、高新技术产品出
口金额同比分别增长43.7%、63.6%，集成电路、自动数据处理设备及零部件
出口分别增长 136.0%、92.9%， 全球AI 算力基础设施建设持续提供需求支撑。
不过，集成电路出口数量仅增长 1.1%，出口金额高增仍较多受到价格因素推
动。 汽车、 家电出口金额分别增长62.0%、31.7%， 出口数量分别增长55.5%、
25.4%，量价共同支撑出口增长；成品油出口金额增长 125.1%，出口动能进
一步扩散。 从进口结构看，集成电路、 自动数据处理设备及零部件进口金额分
别增长 74.7%、296.5%，机床进口金额增长 23.8%，电子产业链和高端制造
相关需求保持较强。 原油进口金额同比增长3.3%， 但进口数量仍下降21.5%，
金额回升主要受到国际油价上涨支撑；铁矿石进口金额、 数量分别下降7.4%、
5.6%，反映传统资源品进口需求偏弱。汽车进口金额和数量分别下降38.1%、
47.2%，国产车型替代效应延续。整体看，9 月外贸增长动能有所扩散，出口
受 AI 产业链、汽车及机电产品共同支撑，进口则呈现高技术产品偏强、部分
资源品及汽车偏弱的结构性分化。 
 外贸表现超预期，进出口双双提速。按美元计，2026 年 1‑ 9 月，我国货物贸
易进出口总额同比增长23.8%， 涨幅相比1‑ 8 月扩大 1.4 个百分点， 持续保持
高位。1‑ 9 月，贸易顺差为 9381.12亿美元，相比 1‑8 月增加 1326.07 亿美
元。按美元计，9 月单月， 货物贸易出口、 进口同比分别增长36.3%、31.0%，
涨幅分别较 8 月回升 11.3 和 2.8 个百分点。9 月出口增长主要受 AI产业链需
求旺盛、 汽车及机电产品出口走强支撑，同时区域产业链贸易联系深化， 对欧
出口也可能存在提前出货现象。进口方面，电子链产品进口保持强势，部分资
源品进口表现分化， 原油进口金额回升则受到国际油价上涨支撑。从景气表现
来看，9 月美欧日制造业景气保持扩张： 标普全球美国制造业PMI 终值为 55.9，
创下自 2022 年 5 月以来的最高水平，产出和新订单以更快的速度扩张；欧元
区制造业 PMI终值上升至 52.9，新订单以自2022年 3月以来最快的速度增长，
出口销售连续第二个月扩大；日本制造业 PMI 终值回落至 54.1，企业记录到
产出和新订单的增长放缓。贸易政策方面，9 月下旬中美就 300 亿美元对等降
税达成框架共识， 但安排尚未落地；降税范围集中于消费品等非敏感品类，高
端制造领域贸易壁垒保持不变。10 月中欧开展贸易磋商，双方就混动汽车达
成贸易谅解，并继续推进电动车反补贴案价格承诺后续程序，有助于降低短期
贸易冲突升级风险。10 月以来，美伊围绕霍尔木兹海峡的紧张局势持续升温，
伊朗加强对海上运输活动限制， 美国持续强化对伊朗石油贸易的封锁，区域供

2
应风险上升。整体看，9月进出口同步提速，出口增长动能相对多元，进口则
更多受到电子产业链需求及大宗商品价格变化影响。预计AI 算力投资、汽车
及机电产品出口将继续提供支撑， 但高基数、 海外需求波动及贸易政策不确定
性可能带来扰动， 部分企业提前出货也可能透支后续需求。进口方面，电子产
业链需求有望延续支撑， 能源及资源品进口则更多取决于国内生产需求和国际
价格变化。
 出口高增覆盖多元贸易伙伴，“抢出口”效应带动对欧出口走强。以美元计，
从贸易总额看，9 月我国与主要贸易伙伴的进出口均有所改善。 对东盟、 欧盟、
美国的进出口总额同比增速分别扩大 13.8、12.0、4.8 个百分点至 45.8%、
16.8%、34.8%。其中，对东盟贸易增速提升幅度较大，反映区域产业链贸易
联系持续深化；对美贸易方面，9 月我国对美进出口总额同比增速有所回升。
中美达成 300 亿美元进口商品关税减让共识， 有望改善后续双边贸易预期， 后
续仍需关注具体降税安排及订单变化。从出口总额看，9 月我国对主要贸易伙

伴出口普遍提速。对东盟、韩国、欧盟的出口同比增速分别扩大22.1、18.9、
18.6 个百分点至 52.3%、68.2%、25.3%。东盟和韩国出口增速显著提升，结
合同期集成电路等电子产品出口高增的表现，全球AI 算力投资和半导体产业
链需求可能是重要支撑。对欧出口增速明显回升，可能受到部分企业提前安排
出货的影响。考虑到欧盟持续讨论限制中国汽车进口，相关贸易政策不确定性
可能促使车企提前交付，带动短期对欧出口增长。从进口总额看，9 月我国对
主要贸易伙伴的进口增速整体变化不大，值得注意的是，对韩国进口同比增速
收窄 13.8 个百分点至 94.3%，但仍处于较高水平。结合韩国半导体出口持续
高增的表现，全球 AI 算力投资带来的芯片需求仍是重要支撑。对韩进口增速
回落可能受到商品结构、价格及基数等因素影响。
 出口增长动能多点扩散，高技术出口景气延续。以美元计，从出口金额看，集
成电路、成品油、自动数据处理设备及零部件、汽车（包括底盘）出口金额同
比分别增长 136.0%、125.1%、92.9%和 62.0%；陶瓷产品、肥料、粮食出口

金额同比分别下降 19.1%、9.2%和 5.3%，其中陶瓷和肥料降幅均有所收窄。
从出口数量看，汽车（包括底盘）、成品油、未锻轧铝及铝材、家用电器出口
数量同比分别增长 55.5%、39.3%、26.1%和 25.4%；此前同比下降的稀土、
肥料、船舶出口数量均转为增长，手机、粮食出口数量则分别下降 12.6%和
7.1%。从出口结构看，9 月机电产品、高新技术产品出口金额同比分别增长
43.7%和 63.6%，占出口总额的比重分别为66.7%和 32.9%，仍是出口增长的
重要支撑。其中，集成电路出口金额同比增长136.0%，出口数量同比仅增长
1.1%， 但已由上月下降转为增长， 出口金额高增仍主要受到价格上涨推动； 自
动数据处理设备及零部件出口金额同比增长92.9%，全球 AI 算力基础设施建
设带来的需求增长仍是重要支撑。从边际变化看， 资源品出口表现分化。 肥料
出口金额同比下降 9.2%，降幅收窄 52.7 个百分点，出口数量同比转为增长
12.7%；成品油出口金额同比增长 125.1%，出口数量同比增长 39.3%，数量

和金额同比涨幅均扩大。劳动密集型产品方面，箱包、鞋靴、陶瓷等产品出口
金额和数量同比表现均有所改善， 但陶瓷出口金额仍下降19.1%。机电产品方
面， 手机和船舶出口金额同比增速分别收窄15.4 和 18.3 个百分点至 14.2%和
2.7%， 但两者出口数量增速均较上月改善。 相比之下， 汽车和家电出口金额同
比增速分别扩大至 62.0%和 31.7%， 出口数量同比增速也分别扩大至55.5%和
25.4%，呈现量价共同支撑的特征。总体而言，9月出口增长由 AI产业链、汽
车、 家电及成品油等多个品类共同支撑， 其中芯片出口金额增长对价格变化较
为敏感，汽车和家电则有较强的实物量支撑。后续来看，外需韧性及 AI 算力
基础设施建设有望继续支撑集成电路、 计算机设备等产品出口，但高基数及海
外需求波动可能对增速形成扰动。
 能源及资源品进口量承压，AI链产品量价现改善。以美元计， 从农产品及能源
进口看，粮食和大豆进口金额同比分别增长6.0%和 2.8%，增速均边际回落；
进口数量同比分别下降2.2%和 7.7%，主要受高基数叠加国内豆粕饲用需求偏

### Page 3

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
3
弱影响。原油进口金额同比由降转升至3.3%，进口数量同比下降 21.5%，降
幅继续收窄。金额与数量走势分化，主要受到国际油价上涨影响， 原油进口实
物量仍处于深度收缩区间。从资源品进口看，9 月铁矿砂及其精矿进口金额、
数量同比分别下降 7.4%和 5.6%，均由升转降，反映黑色产业链相关进口需求
有所走弱。肥料进口金额、数量同比分别增长22.6%和 23.4%，涨幅均较上月
收窄 50 多个百分点，主要是前期进口集中释放之后的正常回调。钢材进口金
额同比增长 4.6%，数量同比下降 10.6%，降幅有所收窄，实物进口量仍然偏
弱。从高技术及机电产品进口看，集成电路进口金额同比增长74.7%，增速收
窄 8.9 个百分点，而进口数量同比仅增长1.7%，金额与数量走势明显分化，
芯片价格上涨及进口产品结构变化是金额高增长的重要原因。 自动数据处理设
备及其零部件进口金额同比增速扩大87.5 个百分点至 296.5%， 反映相关设备
进口金额显著走强。机床进口金额同比由降转升至 23.8%，数量同比增长

7.7%，反映高端制造需求扩张的趋势持续。 汽车进口金额、 数量同比分别下降
38.1%和 47.2%，其中金额降幅扩大、数量降幅收窄，整体进口仍处于明显收
缩区间，反映国产高端新能源车型的替代效应持续显现。整体来看，9 月进口
延续结构性分化， 电子设备及高端制造相关产品进口走强， 能源及部分资源品
进口偏弱，汽车进口持续收缩。展望后续，高技术及高端制造相关产品有望成
为进口增长的重要支撑， 能源及资源品进口仍取决于国内需求与国际价格， 汽
车进口则继续面临国产车型的替代压力。
 风险提示：国内政策落地不及预期，海外经济波动超预期， 地缘政治风险超预
期。

分析师承诺  
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明  
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌 幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准， 新三板市场以三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。  
公司评级  
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上  
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -10%与 10%之间  
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -20%与-10%之间  
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 -20%以下  
行业评级  
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%以下

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: Given its longstanding position in the global foundry industry, 
ever challenging TSMC’s leadership during the late 1990s, we believe 
UMC stands well to benefit from a ripple effect in demand spurred by AI 
scaling, as detailed in the main section of this report. We expect UMC’s 
wafer loadings to reach 100% from 1Q27, with price hikes to go through 
(see Price hike to go through? Still likely, 31 July). That said, we believe the 
equity market has priced in this AI strength on extra volume and pricing 
power for UMC. Hence, we stick with our Hold (3) rating on the counter.

What’s the impact: 3Q26 preview and 4Q26 outlook. We expect UMC to 
report 3Q26 net profit of TWD16.4bn (+9% YoY), varying not much from the 
Bloomberg consensus. Its monthly revenue finished at c.TWD49bn QTD, 
reaching 65% of our/consensus 3Q26 estimates, which we consider 
tracking inline. For 4Q26, we expect UMC to earn net profit of TWD16bn, 
13% below consensus, as the street seems to be assuming higher non-
operating gains. UMC is scheduled to report results on 28 October; we will 
be offering updates in due course.

<=28nm ramp on AI scaling and fab optimisation. UMC’s 28/22nm-and-
below revenue contribution has hovered around the high-30% range over 
the past 2 years, after meaningful ramp during the COVID period on 
broadened demand applications from display drivers and TV SoCs to 
power management. With AI scaling creating a ripple effect in companion 
chip demand for connectivity (ie, silicon photonics) and power (ie, server 
PMICs), we believe UMC is well positioned to benefit from any spillover 
demand, with the strength augmented by TSMC’s fab optimisation, which 
could strengthen UMC’s pricing power. Given that UMC’s wafer loadings 
are likely to reach 100% from 1Q27, we expect its price hikes to go through 
and model increases of 2-5%, effective from 1Q27, across nodes related to 
AI applications. This should resume its <=28nm ramp to exceed a half of 
revenue in 2028. All in, we raise our 2027-28E EPS by 22%.

What we recommend: Despite our forecast revisions, we remain 
comfortable with our Hold (3) rating for UMC, as we believe its AI strength 
is already priced in. We raise our 12M TP to TWD145 (previous: TWD108), 
based on our higher ROE-adjusted PBR target of 4.2x (previous: 3.3x) 
applied to 1-year-forward BVPS on a higher ROE trajectory. Upside/ 
downside risks would be any price hikes above our expectations/ 
geopolitical overhangs (war, FX, interest rate) weighing on end-demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS do not vary much from consensus, 
indicating that UMC’s AI strength is already reflected in its valuation.

9 October 2026

United Microelectronics

A beneficiary of the AI ripple effect, but …

➢ UMC looks set to benefit from AI demand spillover 
➢ Yet we believe the AI ripple effect is being priced in 
➢ Maintain our Hold (3) call, with a higher 12M TP of TWD145

Company profile 
Incorporated in Taiwan, United Microelectronics Corp (UMC) is one of the leading semiconductor 
foundries in the world and second largest in Taiwan. UMC offers a series of foundry services from 
photo-mask, wafer fabrication to wafer probe & test. Its wafer fabs are spread over Taiwan, China and 
Singapore, with technology offerings ranging from 0.25u and above, 0.18/0.15u, 0.13u, 90nm, 65nm, 
45/40nm, 28/22nm to 14nm FF.

United Microelectronics (2303 TT): 9 October 2026 
ESG analysis

Update Date: 8 Oct 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company 
ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 1
UMC’s Board of Directors consists of nine members whose expertise covers industry, academia,
finance, accounting, and management. Among these directors, six are independent directors,
representing 67% of total directors in 2024. The percentage of independent directors is high
relative to the regulatory requirement of at least 20% or at least 2 independent directors.
Although some of UMC's executive management are also directors, UMC's Chairman and
President are not the same person. Considering this and its relative-high percentage of
independent directors, we believe the board can perform its functionalities well
Capital management 2 It kept its dividend payout ratios above 50% in 2017-25, which is at a healthy, sustainable level.Overall, we see the balance between investment and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 1 Sales to related parties were around 1-2% of its total revenue each year. The percentage isinsignificant and thus we see limited risk for UMC.
S Supply chainmanagement 2
UMC has constructed an evaluation framework for reviewing the sustainable development of its
supply chains based on UMC’s GG-026-004-C sustainable supply-chain management review
guidelines to ensure its suppliers fulfil environmental protection, social responsibility, labour and
human rights, safety & health and sustainable development guidelines. We believe these efforts
could reduce its supply-chain risk.
E Water & wastewatermanagement 2
UMC developed its “Water Management Policy and Commitment” in 2015 to promote more
effective utilisation of water resources. In line with this policy, UMC continues to enhance
recycling through increased use of reclaimed and desalinated water, ongoing wastewater
treatment R&D, and optimised discharge management. In 2024, the company achieved a 30.3%
reduction in water withdrawal per unit product, surpassing its 14% goal and meeting its 2030
target ahead of schedule, which demonstrates UMC’s strong commitment to water conservation.
E
Waste & hazardous
materials
management
2
To achieve the goal of zero pollution, UMC has established a comprehensive new material
evaluation process to effectively screen and control new materials introduced into the
development of new processes. Through this effort, it can avoid or reduce the use of raw
materials and the associated environmental impact. UMC was the first semiconductor
manufacturer company in the world to obtain QC-080000 IECQ Hazardous Substance Process
Management (HSPM) certification for all its fabs by leading global semiconductor manufacturers
in completing the third-party verification and audit of its HSPM system in 2006.
E GHG emissions 2
UMC was the first semiconductor company in Taiwan to pass integrated environmental
protection, safety and health management system certifications in 2001. UMC has established
the UMC Low Carbon Commitment as a guide for future carbon reduction projects. Its GHG
emission reduction goals include: 1) to reduce fluorinated greenhouse gas emissions intensity by
65% over 2013-25 vs. 2010, and 2) to reduce electricity consumption intensity by 15% in 10
years over 2016-25 vs. 2015.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

We are positive on Largan and expect its strong alignment 
technology to continue supporting its expansion from handset lens 
into FA and FAU for CPO switches. The company’s FA has passed 
customer qualification with strong positive feedback, while 
customers prefer to purchase its integrated product (FA+FAU), 
reaffirming our positive view on Largan’s business expansion. 
Management highlights that its strong alignment and automated 
production provide strong precision, fast production and testing, 
supporting customer penetration. We model FAU revenue 
contribution at 3% in 2027E and above 50% in 2030E, driving 
Largan’s earnings growth, margin expansion, and valuation 
re-rating. Maintain Buy with TP up to NT$11,000 (implies 39.8x 
2028E PE).

3-month revenue preview: Largan delivered weak revenue growth 
in 3Q26 with revenue -11% YoY, which we attribute to a slow 
smartphone market amid high memory costs, fewer model launches 
of its major smartphone brand customer, and foldable phones 
released in 4Q26 (report link). The miss also reflects a slower 
handset lens pixel mix upgrade than we had previously estimated.

### Page 3

We expect Oct revenues to be at a similar level to Sep, and to sequentially decline in Nov 
and Dec, resulting in 4Q26 revenue growth of +7% QoQ.

Earnings revision

Our net income estimates are revised by -13% / -4% / +16% in 2026-28E mainly on 
changes in revenues, GM, and Opex ratio, reflecting: (1) handset lens: we continue to 
expect Largan’s lens pixel mix upgrade, but at a slower pace, reflecting the 3Q26 miss 
and slow smartphone market amid high memory costs, (2) FAU: we estimate revenue 
contribution will begin in 2H27, with 0.6m / 5m in 2027 / 28E, along with product mix 
upgrade toward higher bandwidth FAU that should support ASP and GM. We expect FAU 
revenue contribution of 3% in 2027E and exceed 50% in 2030E, (3) GM: we lower our 
GM estimates to reflect the slower pace of handset lens pixel mix upgrade and the 
company’s higher costs on green energy. We expect GM to sustain at ~50% in 2026-28E, 
supported by continuous handset lens pixel mix upgrade and strong demand of FAU, (4) 
Opex ratio: we continue to expect rising opex, while the opex ratio would improve as 
strong revenue growth driven by FAU and FAU’s higher ASP relative to handset lens 
enhance operating efficiency.

### Page 9

Price Target Risks and Methodology - Largan
Valuation: We are Buy rated on Largan and have a 12-month target price of NT$11,000,
which is based on discounted PE. Our target P/E multiple of 29.8x is derived from (1)
peers’ avg. ratio of trading PE to forward year fundamentals (NI YoY and OPM), which is
at 0.5x, and (2) Largan’s forward year fundamentals: 2030-31E NI YoY at 19% on avg.
and OPM at 41% on avg. We apply the target PE multiple to Largan’s 2030E EPS and

discount it back to 2027E via a COE of 10.7%, which is based on beta at 1.1x, risk free at
3.5%, and market risk premium at 6.5%. Our target price of NT$11,000 implies 39.8x
2028E PE, the first full year of FAU contribution, which is within Largan’s historical
trading range of 8.0x to 45.1x, reflecting our positive view on Largan’s expansion from
handset lens to FAU, which broadens its exposure from consumer electronics to AI data
center that enjoys strong market growth, and could support a valuation re-rating. We

note that Taiwan AI supply chain also enjoys re-rating when expanding from consumer
electronics to AI data center. Maintain Buy.
Key risks: (1) slower-than-expected smartphone market growth, (2) fiercer competition
in handset lenses, (3) slower-than-expected smartphone camera lens specification
upgrades or Largan’s handset lens pixel mix upgrade, (4) slower-than-expected FAU
ramp up, and (5) fiercer competition in FAU.
9 October 2026   9
Goldman Sachs Largan (3008.TW)For the exclusive use of KEVINLU@LENOVO.COM

Global Research
Largan Precision
Core weakness outweighs progress in AI optics
CPO progress is more tangible but substantial success is already priced in
We maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target price as erosion in the core smartphone
business outweighs CPO progress. The Q326 call confirmed that Largan's fiber array has
passed customer qualification although its customer qualified another vendor's prism
micro-lens assembly (PMLA) that Largan would now buy in. If Largan qualifies the full

FAU plus carrier assembly for 2H27, content rises but with more pass-through cost and
less margin upside. We roll our valuation to 2028E, the first full year of CPO production,
though 25% below the NT$6,660 close on October 8.
Full FAU assembly would lift content, but with more pass-through
We believe Largan's FA customer is one of two  module suppliers on NVIDIA's 2H27
program, which we estimate could reach 40% share. The program is still subject to

successful yield ramp and could move to metalens in 2028, which would reopen supplier
qualification. Assembling the full FAU could lift Largan's ASP to US$125-150 leveraging
its precision alignment and automation but the PMLA, V-groove and carrier parts would
be pass through that cuts into margin leverage. We expand our FAU TAM analysis
(Figure 9S u m m a r y  o f  F A U  d r i v e r s :  1 2 . 3 m n / 3 5 . 6 m n  b a s e  c a s e  i n  2 0 2 8 - 2 0 3 0) with FAU market in 2028/30 expanding to 12.3mn/35.6mn units, with Largan

base case set at 25% of the TAM.
Core smartphone business stays soft into 2027
Q326 sales of NT$15.7bn, +15% QoQ/-11% YoY missedUBSe/Street +36%/+33% on
weaker flagship smartphone demand. GMs fell 7.2ppts QoQ to 42.2%, lowest in 13
years on renewable energy costs and low variable-aperture yields, dropping OpM to 17
year lows at 30.3% and EPS to NT$39.6, below UBS/street NT$51.50/NT$49.69. We cut
Q4 to NT$16.7bn +7% QoQ, 11% below consensus as its customer trimmed orders

after a pricier iPhone 18 launch and possible lag to Genius which is picking up periscope
share and outgrew in Q3 (+1% YoY vs. -11% YoY). We cut 2027 from NT$77bn to NT
$67bn, before a recovery to NT$79bn in 2028 with the next periscope upgrade. We see
GM recovering to 47.5%/48.9% in 2027-28 as yields improve and energy costs pass
through in 2028 pricing.
Maintain Sell and our NT$5,000 target
We cut 2026E/27E EPS by 14%/16% to NT$160/NT$180 and introduce 2028E EPS of

NT$220 for the core smartphone business. We value the lens business at NT$3,300 (15x
2028E ex-CPO EPS of NT$220, the middle of Largan's 13–18x historical range) and CPO
option at NT$1,700 or US$7bn (49x our base-case 2028E CPO EPS of NT$35, near the
top of optical peers' 14–54x). Our base case gives Largan 25% of a 12.3mn-unit, US
$1.85bn FAU market at US$150 per unit and a 50% gross margin. That credits it with
yields and margins it still needs to prove in a quarter when yields and energy costs cut

7.2ppts from its lens margin. At ~NT$6,660, the shares imply ~NT$69 of 2028E CPO
EPS, about twice our base case.

Core smartphone lens seeing weakness with declining

not offset the flagship decline.

CPO fiber array qualified, but rising pass-through as it upgrades to FAU
production
Largan's fiber array (FA) has passed qualification with a volume customer, and
management expects mass-production capacity by mid-2027 in a newly completed fab
dedicated to FA/FAU, using in-house equipment. The company's next step is to produce
the full detachable FAU mounted on a carrier, a board with three alignment beams that
sits on the optical engine's receptor. Largan can build FAUs now and says the customer

### Page 3

would prefer it to supply the full assembly, but qualification is pending with the
customer preferring FAU and carrier sampled separately.
Even as its potential to supply the full FAU improve, Largan will buy in most of the
content. The customer has qualified another PMLA vendor and prefers semiconductor-
made optics; V-grooves and carrier beams and boards are outsourced and the customer
does not want Largan to make MT ferrules. Management described its role as closer to a

contractor and said margins hinge on yields, as the FA carries a high ASP but also a large
bill of materials.  Supplying the complete FAU could raise billed content, but a larger
purchased bill of materials also dilutes the relationship between revenue and value
added. Our full-FAU ASP assumption requires Largan to capture that invoice scope.
Next year's product is grating-coupled and works for both CPO and NPO while edge-
coupled designs are being explored with the customer. The current product does not go

directly to end customers, but management expects to work directly with end customers
on next-generation designs (likely 2028) as it progresses toward multi-row FAs.
To ramp production, Largan has bought several land parcels and will build new fabs from
scratch, with construction finishing around 2030. It may outsource some capacity in the
interim and said it cannot yet be sure capacity will meet demand.
Annual FAU TAM reaches US$1.85bn in 2028 and US$4.5bn in 2030 in

our base case
We update our FAU (fiber array unit) TAM factoring CPO/NPO penetration into
AI networking switches, NVIDIA multi-rack systems, scale-up on compute and switch
boards, and non-NVIDIA accelerators.  We assume one passive FAU assembly connecting
a fiber array to a photonic engine with one unit per optical engine.
We assume one FAU per optical engine and 32 per switch ASIC in our base case,
matching NVIDIA's 32x3.2T engines on its 102.4T Spectrum-X package. Broadcom's

102.4Tb/s Davisson uses 16 engines at 6.4Tb/s, so our bear case assumes and even mix
of these two designs (24 per ASIC). We note that higher switch bandwidth does not
automatically increase FAU count if engine bandwidth rises at the same rate. Our 2030
assumption maintains the 32 FAUs per switch based on bandwidth rising to 204.8Tb/s
ASICs using 6.4Tb/s optical engines.
Figure 4: UBS scenario based on 32 FAUs per switch although switches to date carry a range of specifications

Our base case for the FAU market sums up 4 opportunities for CPO/NPO attach:
1. Scale-out and scale-across switches
We estimate annual switch ASIC shipments x CPO + NPO penetration x optical engines
per ASIC x FAUs per engine. In 2028, 25% CPO/NPO penetration or 1.0m 102.4T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs each produces 8.0m FAUs. In 2030, 55% of 1.1mn 204.8T switch
ASICs at 32 FAUs drives a market size of 19.4mn FAUs.

2. NVIDIA's multi-rack connections for Rubin Ultra and Feynman
NVIDIA at its 2026 GTC San Jose proposed a multi-rack option to network together 8
Oberon NVL72 racks for the Rubin Ultra generation and 8 Kyber NVL144 racks.
Feedback at the time showed  possible transceiver or NPO connections for Rubin Ultra

and CPO connections for Feynman.  Our 2028 base case uses 110,000 racks, 15% multi-
rack adoption and 75% CPO/NPO penetration, giving approximately 12,400 connected
racks.
For 2030, our rack count falls to 35,000 racks as we assume Kyber would have 4x the
number of GPUs, equivalent to 140k Oberon equivalents. We assume the 35,000 racks
at 55% multi-rack adoption x 80% CPO/NPO attach gives 15,400 connected racks. We
assume about 216 and 576 inter-rack FAUs per rack, respectively, driving a market

opportunity of 2.7mn FAUs in 2028 and 8.9mn in 2030.

Overall market opportunity: 12.3mn/35.6mn generates a US$1.85bn/US
$4.5bn TAM in 2028/2030
We sum four pools: scale-out/scale-across switches, NVIDIA multi-rack pods, CPO native

NVIDIA GPUs and non-NVIDIA scale-up.  We add 10%/5% for FAUs lost at downstream
attach and test and for field spares, driving a market size of in our base case of 12.3mn
FAUs in 2028 and 35.6mn in 2030. At US$150/US$125 per FAU, that is a US$1.85bn
market in 2028 and US$4.5bn in 2030 (bear/bull US$0.5bn/US$3.5bn in 2028; US
$1.4bn/US$9.0bn in 2030). The expansion to 2030 comes from wider optical adoption
and greater content in scale-up systems, despite an assumed FAU ASP decline from US

$150 to US$125.

Scenario analysis on Largan's CPO opportunity
Our base case gives Largan the benefit of the doubt for successfully ramping up a full
FAU production and capturing 25% of the overall FAU TAM, yielding NT$35 of 2028E
EPS, about 16% of our 2028E lens EPS. A 25% share means 3.1mn FAUs in its first full
year of production: about 29% of NVIDIA-linked units and roughly three-quarters of the

allocation our checks suggest its module partner holds. We book these at the full US
$150 FAU price and 50% gross margin, allowing for an early-supply premium even
though the optics, V-grooves and carrier parts are bought in. That gives 2028E CPO sales
of US$462mn, rising to US$1.1bn and NT$84 of EPS in 2030 on a larger market.
Our bear case (15% share, 45% GM) gives NT$5 of CPO EPS in 2028E and NT$14 in
2030E; our bull case (35% share, 55% GM) gives NT$103 and NT$267. Each 5ppt of

share moves 2028E CPO EPS by ~NT$6.9 (~NT$340/share at 49x), and each 5ppt of
gross margin by ~NT$4.6 (~NT$225/share).  If gross margin were 35% rather than 50%,
holding operating expenses at 12% of sales, EPS would fall to approximately NT$21. A
next-generation shift in lens or coupling design could also change content and
alignment requirements including a potential metalens design which could invite in
different suppliers.
Our 2030 base case implies ~8.9mn Largan FAUs a year (21mn in the upside case),

though with risks around competition in a crowded field, yields and technology. A shift
to semiconductor-made optics or metalens would loosen alignment tolerances and
erode the precision premium behind Largan's pitch. The upside lies in direct work with
cloud providers on multi-row FAs from 2028, where Largan's multi-layer alignment is
most differentiated.

Valuation and investment implications
We roll our valuation forward to 2028E and value Largan on a sum of the parts. We
value the lens business at NT$3,300 (15x 2028E EPS, the middle of Largan's 13–18x

historical range) and CPO at NT$1,700 (49x our base-case 2028E CPO EPS of NT$35,
near the top of optical peers' 14–54x). Our base case gives Largan 25% of a 12.3mn-
unit, US$1.85bn FAU market at US$150 per unit and a 50% gross margin. The 49x
multiple also assigns a substantial value to a business whose complete assembly still
requires qualification and whose margins still need to demonstrate can reach similar
levels to its mature smartphone business. At ~NT$6,660 recent share price level, the

shares imply ~NT$69 of 2028E CPO EPS, about twice our base case.
Upside risks include faster full-FAU qualification, firm volume commitments from
additional customers, stronger lens demand and better yields. Near-term catalysts are
the customer's capacity forecast and its visit to the new fab in the coming month. A
large forecast or faster FAU plus carrier qualification would be upside risks to our sell.

Forecast returns
Forecast price appreciation -24.9%
Forecast dividend yield 1.2%
Forecast stock return -23.7%
Market return assumption 6.3%
Forecast excess return -30.0%
Company Description
Founded in 1987, Largan Precision (Largan) is one of the world's leading manufacturers of
lenses used in cameras and IT products. It specialises in plastic/hybrid lenses and, to a lesser
extent, glass lenses and modules. In 2022, 20MP+ camera lenses were 10-20% of Largan's

shipment mix, 10MP lenses were 50-60%, 8MP lenses were 0-10% and other lenses were
30-40%.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
Our price target is based on SOTP valuation on the CPO opportunity and traditional lens
business.
We think Largan's downside risks include: 1) ongoing pricing pressure from smartphone OEM
clients and increasingly intense competition among lens suppliers; and 2) a slower-than-
expected smartphone demand recovery in China, and 3) CPO commercial success below the

market's elevated expectations.
We believe the key upside risk is a faster-than-expected CPO qualification process at Largan.
In addition, if the company demonstrates exceptionally efficient and highly accurate
automation capabilities, it could support greater market share gains within the industry than
we currently anticipate.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the

Greater China Technology Hardware  | Asia Pacific
Takeaways on NPO from 2026
OCP Global Summit
Morgan Stanley Asia Limited+
Andy Meng, CFA
Equity Analyst
Andy.Meng@morganstanley.com                                                +852 2239-7689
Betty Chen
Research Associate
Betty.H.Chen@morganstanley.com                                                +852 2239-7213
Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
On Day 2 of the 2026 OCP Global Summit, an IPEC optical interconnect workshop

focused on discussions around NPO. Industry participants such as Source Photonics,
Lightcounting, Alibaba, Meta, Oracle, Microsoft, Ciena, Ligent, Genuine Optics,
Lumentum, Lumilens, NVIDIA and Huawei presented their views on NPO.
The industry has formed the view connectivity, rather than compute alone, is
becoming the primary bottleneck. Near-packaged optics (NPO) move optical
conversion closer to compute, targeting better signal integrity, bandwidth, and

energy efficiency. OpenCPX explicitly targets common mechanical, electrical,
optical, thermal, and management interfaces across suppliers, providing the
ecosystem with a shared implementation framework rather than only a broad
architectural vision.
However, we also observed debate over whether pluggable NPO offers materially
better flexibility and serviceability than CPO. Key concern on CPO is that optical
failure could result in scrapping an entire ASIC assembly.

Overall, we observed growing discussion and interests in NPO, implying a greater
likelihood of accelerated commercialization over the next 12-18 months, in our view.
Morgan Stanley does and seeks to do business with
companies covered in Morgan Stanley Research. As a result,
investors should be aware that the firm may have a conflict of
interest that could affect the objectivity of Morgan Stanley
Research. Investors should consider Morgan Stanley
Research as only a single factor in making their investment

communications with a subject company, public appearances
and trading securities held by a research analyst account.
Key Takeaways
Connectivity has become the bottleneck, and NPO is a potential solution. We
observed growing discussion and interest in NPO at the conference.
October 14, 2026 08:47 AM GMT

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

9 月和三季度经济数据点评

内需出现向好迹象   
9 月经济数据中，固投累计同比增速和社零当月同比增速均好于万得一致预期，工业
增加值当月同比增速符合万得一致预期且维持在相对较高水平。 
◼ 2026 年三季度 GDP 实际同比增速 4.5%，三季度名义 GDP 同比增速 6.0%；前三
季度实际 GDP 累计同比增长 4.6%，名义 GDP 累计同比增长 5.6%。 
◼ 9 月工业增加值同比增长 5.5%。1-9 月采矿业工业增加值累计同比增速 2.0%，制
造业累计同比增速 5.7%，公用事业累计同比增速 5.4%，高技术产业累计同比增
速 14.3%。 
◼ 9 月社零同比增长 1.1%， 其中， 除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长3.9%。 限额
以上商品消费中，算力消费品依旧主导社零，金价下跌推动金银珠宝消费，房地
产后周期消费短期未见好转。 
◼ 1-9 月固定资产投资累计同比增速下降 7.2%，民间固定资产投资累计同比下降
10.4%， 扣除房地产开发投资，1-9 月全国固定资产投资下降 4.2%， 民间投资下降
6.7%。 分主要行业看， 制造业投资1-9 月累计同比下降 2.1%， 基建投资累计同比
下降 6.1%，地产投资累计同比下降 20.6%。 
◼ 1-9 月住宅投资增速下降 20.5%， 商品房销售面积同比下降13.2%， 商品房销售额
累计同比下降 13.9%。2026 年 9 月，70 个大中城市新建商品住宅价格指数同比下
降 3.0%，较 8 月上升 0.3 个百分点，二手住宅价格指数下降 4.8%，较 8 月上升
0.3 个百分点。 
◼ 2026 年前三季度全国居民人均可支配收入 34230 元，同比增长 5.3%，居民人均
消费支出 22354 元，同比增长 2.7%。2026 年前三季度居民边际消费倾向较上半
年小幅上升，但整体来看居民消费意愿较弱，边际消费倾向和增量收入边际消费
倾向对比 2025 年同期均有所下降，特别是增量收入边际消费倾向降幅较大。 
◼ 三季度经济增速超预期，全年稳增长压力下降。三季度实际 GDP 同比增长 4.5%
超出市场预期，带动前三季度实际 GDP 累计同比增速达到 4.6%，略高于全年增
速目标区间下限，加之去年四季度经济增长基数偏低，一定程度上缓解了短期稳
增长的压力。从 9 月经济数据看，稳增长的政策起到了一定效果：工业增加值同
比增速符合市场预期，同时维持在较高增速上；社零同比增速较 8 月明显上升，
表明内需仍有平稳释放的潜力；固定资产投资累计同比降幅持平于 1-8 月，但从
我们计算的当月增速看， 基建投资和制造业投资增速均较8 月出现较大幅度好转。
从季节规律看，四季度经济增长仍有支撑：一是生产端补库存，可能驱动工业增
加值同比增速维持在当前的增速水平，考虑到 PPI 同比增速走高和 9 月出口交货
值增速走高，价格因素和外需可能成为四季度生产端超预期的推动力；二是四季
度是传统消费旺季，从社零环比增速看，在 9 月环比高增长的基础上，四季度的
三个月在电商促销和春节前消费等因素推动下，环比仍能维持较强的正增长；三
是四季度也是海外消费旺季，进出口或依赖我国生产端优势，持续受益于外需拉
动。 
◼ 经济向“新”带动政策求“变”。 8 月底至今，从稳增长的角度出发，政策做了
多方面调整：一是国家发改委等部门召开全国投资工作推进会议，部署七项稳投
资工作，二是住建部等三部门推进商品住房销售制度改革，稳定地产预期，三是
证监会发文支持构建房地产发展新模式，四是央行调整完善几项货币政策工具稳
增长， 五是财政部安排使用地方政府债务结存限额5500 亿元， 六是中共中央、 国
务院印发《关于发展新质生产力的意见》，继续大力支持经济转型升级等等。我
们认为虽然 2026 年年内稳增长的压力减轻， 但从中长期看， 经济增长的挑战依然
很大： 从全球范围内看， 包括但不限于AI 技术发展对生产关系的冲击、 地缘局势
变化对能源和通胀的影响、 经济增长K 型分化在行业之间和国别之间带来的发展
不均衡、各经济体面临的增长挑战与财政压力、发达国家货币政策收缩与金融条
件收紧对全球资本市场的外溢性影响等等；从国内看，我们面临的挑战依然是以
维持经济向新向优趋势和供强需弱的现实矛盾为主。我们认为未来一段时间内，
我国宏观经济的主要表现或仍是增速缓步下台阶，因此积极的财政政策和适度宽
松的货币政策发力重点或更多聚焦于托底民生和实现社会公平，其次是发展先进
生产力，然后才是稳增长。尽管国内经济看起来只是“平稳”，但我们认为相较
于其他经济体，我国的比较优势依然非常明显。 
◼ 风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济超预期回落；国际局势复杂化。

### Page 2

2026 年 10 月 19 日 9 月和三季度经济数据点评 2

2026 年三季度实际 GDP 同比增长 4.5%  
2026 年三季度 GDP 实际同比增速 4.5%，较2026 年二季度增速上升 0.2 个百分点； 三季度名义GDP
同比增速 6.0%，较 2026 年二季度增速上升 0.1 个百分点。前三季度实际 GDP 累计同比增长 4.6%，
名义 GDP 累计同比增长 5.6%。 
前三季度平减指数累计同比上升 0.99%,较上半年平减指数累计同比上升 0.29 个百分点。三季度平减
指数上升，主要还是受 PPI 同比增速处于较高水平的拉动。

图表 1. GDP 同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券

三季度第二产业和第三产业实际 GDP 当季同比增速均较二季度有所上升 。 
分产业看 ，2026 年三季度第一产业实际 GDP 当季同比增长 3.6%， 增速较二季度下降0.1 个百分点 ，
第二产业实际 GDP 当季同比增长 3.4%，增速较二季度上升 0.4 个百分点，第三产业实际 GDP 当季
同比增长 5.2%，增速较二季度上升 0.1 个百分点。 
从三次产业对 GDP 累计同比贡献率看，2026 年前三季度,第三产业的贡献率为 65.9%,较上半年下降
0.2 个百分点，其次是第二产业的贡献率 29.5%,较上半年下降 0.7 个百分点，较上半年贡献率上升的
主要是第一产业，贡献率 4.6%，上升 0.9 个百分点；与 2023 年-2025 年三次产业的贡献率平均水平
相比，2026 年前三季度第三产业对 GDP 累计同比的贡献率偏高 4.0 个百分点，第二产业贡献率偏低
3.8 个百分点。

### Page 3

2026 年 10 月 19 日 9 月和三季度经济数据点评 3

工业增加值同比增速维持在较高水平 
9 月工业增加值同比增长 5.5%，较 8 月上升 0.3 个百分点，较去年同期下降 1.0 个百分点，基本持平
于万得一致预期的 5.52%。

图表 4. 工业增加值同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券

从环比增速来看，9 月工业增加值环比上升 0.61%，与 2016 年-2019 年期间同期平均水平 0.56%相比
偏高,略低于去年同期的环比增速 0.65%，9 月工业增加值环比较 8 月上行,符合季节性规律。 
从行业分类来看，1-9 月采矿业工业增加值累计同比增速 2.0%，制造业累计同比增速 5.7%，公用事
业累计同比增速 5.4%， 高技术产业累计同比增速14.3%。 从企业类型来看 ，1-9 月国企工业增加值同
比增速 3.8%，股份制企业同比增速 5.8%，外企工业增加值同比增速 3.2%，私企工业增加值同比增
速 4.3%。与 1-8 月相比，1-9 月累计同比增速上升的是公共事业、高技术产业和外企,下降的是采矿
业和私企。9 月当月工业增加值同比增速相较 8 月，各行业和企业类型中工业增加值同比增速上升
的是采矿业、公共事业、国企、股份制、外企和私企，下降的是高技术产业和制造业 。 
1-9 月，装备制造业增加值同比增长 10.2%，高技术制造业增加值增长 14.3%，分别快于全部规模以
上工业增加值 4.9 和 9.0 个百分点；分产品看，3D 打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器人产品产量同
比分别增长 62.5%、39.5%、29.1%。

图表 5. 工业增加值环比增速  图表 6. 主要行业工业增加值当月同比增速

### Page 4

2026 年 10 月 19 日 9 月和三季度经济数据点评 4

9 月，采矿业和大宗周期品加工业当月工业增加值同比增速较 8 月增速相对较强，但受上游原材料
涨价影响，中游设备制造业工业增加值同比增速表现偏弱 。 
采矿业方面，1-9 月煤炭开采和洗选业增加值累计同比增长 0.2%，石油和天然气开采业增加值累计
同比增长 5.7%；从当月同比增速来看，较 8 月增速上升幅度均超过 2.0 个百分点。 
消费类制造业方面，1-9 月农副食品加工业增加值累计同比增长 4.6%，纺织业增加值累计同比增长
3.2%，医药制造业增加值累计同比增长 5.2%；从当月同比增速来看，较 8 月增速下降的是农副食品
加工业和医药制造业。 
大宗周期品加工业方面，1-9 月化学原料及化学制品制造业增加值累计同比增长 3.6%，非金属矿物
制品业增加值累计同比下降 2.9%， 黑色金属冶炼及压延加工业增加值累计同比增长1.7%， 有色金属
冶炼及压延加工业增加值累计同比下降 1.6%；从当月同比增速来看，较 8 月增速下降的仅有有色金
属，化学原料和非金属矿物当月工业增加值增速均较 8 月升幅超过 1.0 个百分点。 
中游设备制造业方面，1-9 月通用设备制造业增加值累计同比增长 8.2%，专用设备制造业增加值累
计同比增长 9.5%， 汽车制造业增加值累计同比增长7.3%， 电气机械及器材制造业增加值累计同比增
长 7.1%，计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业增加值累计同比增长 15.8%；从当月同比增速来看，
较 8 月增速上升的仅有专用设备，汽车制造、电气设备和通用设备增速较 8 月降幅均超过 1.0 个百
分点。

### Page 5

2026 年 10 月 19 日 9 月和三季度经济数据点评 5

9 月外需延续较强走势，出口交货值同比大幅上升，产销率小幅上升。 
9 月规模以上工业企业产销率为 97.0%，较 8 月上升 0.2 个百分点，较去年同期上升 0.3 个百分点，
当月同比增速下降 0.2%；1-9 月规模以上工业企业产销率为 96.4%，较去年同期上升 0.5 个百分点，
累计同比增长 0.1 个百分点。 
9 月规模以上工业企业出口交货值同比增长 17.0%，较 8 月上升 5.9 个百分点，1-9 月规模以上工业
企业出口交货值累计同比增长 10.7%，较 1-8 月上升 0.7 个百分点。

图表 11. 产销率当月值和累计同比增速  图表 12. 出口交货值同比增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

社零增速明显上升 
9 月社零同比增长 1.1%，较8 月上升 0.7 个百分点 ， 其中 ， 除汽车以外的消费品零售额同比增长3.9%，
较 8 月上升 1.4 个百分点；1-9 月社零累计同比增长 1.1%，其中，服务消费累计同比增长 5.0%，较
1-8 月上升 0.1 个百分点。 
9 月社零环比增长 0.4%，虽然较历史同期平均水平明显偏低，但较去年同期环比增速上升 0.5 个百
分点。

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

1-9 月网上商品和服务零售额累计同比增长 4.4%，增速较 1-8 月下降 0.2 个百分点，其中实物商品网
上零售额累计同比增长 3.8%， 增速较1-8 月下降 0.5 个百分点 。1-9 月网上零售额累计占比为 40.9%，
较 2025 年同期上升 10.1 个百分点，较 1-8 月下降 0.2 个百分点。 
9 月商品消费同比增长 1.0%，较 8 月上升 0.7 个百分点，限额以上商品消费同比下降 2.5%，较 8 月
上升 1.5 个百分点；9 月餐饮消费同比增长 2.1%，较 8 月上升 1.0 个百分点，限额以上餐饮消费同比
增长 2.0%，较 8 月上升 2.7 个百分点。前三季度，在服务零售额中，通讯信息服务类、旅游咨询租
赁服务类、文体休闲服务类零售额较快增长。 
1-9 月限额以上企业消费品零售总额累计同比下降 1.5%， 限额以上企业商品消费累计同比下降1.8%,
弱于餐饮消费累计同比增速 1.3%。

9 月算力消费品依旧主导社零，金价下跌推动金银珠宝消费，房地产后周期消费短期未见好转 。 
从产品分类来看，9 月限额以上企业商品零售总额增速较高的细分行业包括通讯器材类（31.1%）、
烟酒类（13.8%）和文化办公用品类（7.9%），增速较低的细分行业包括汽车类（-21.2%）、金银珠
宝类 （-9.6%） 和建筑及装潢材料类 （-9.2%） ；与8 月相比，增速上升较多的有金银珠宝类、石油及
制品类和家具类，增速下降的仅有汽车类和家用电器。

2026 年 10 月 19 日 9 月和三季度经济数据点评 8

图表 21. 固定资产投资环比增速的季节规律  图表 22. 固投各项构成分类的累计同比 增速

资料来源：万得，中银证券  资料来源：万得，中银证券

9 月制造业和基建当月投资增速较 8 月开始回升。 
从产业分类来看，第一产业累计同比下降 3.9%，第二产业累计同比下降 2.7%，第三产业累计同比下
降 10.0%； 从地区来看 ， 东部累计同比下降10.3%， 中部下降11.1%， 西部下降8.7%， 东北下降26.0%；
从企业类型来看，内资企业累计同比下降 7.0%，国有控股同比下降 3.2%，外商投资下降 6.7%。 
分主要行业看，制造业投资 1-9 月累计同比下降 2.1%，基建投资累计同比下降 6.1%，地产投资累计
同比下降 20.6%。从我们测算的当月同比增速看，9 月制造业投资同比增速下降 0.9%，较 8 月上升
5.1 个百分点，基建投资同比增速下降 10.4%，较 8 月上升 4.3 个百分点，房地产投资同比增速下降
27.2%，较 8 月下降 1.0 个百分点。 
1-9 月固投增速较高的一级分类行业包括采矿业（3.2%）、交运仓储（1.9%）和制造业（-2.1%），
固投增速较低的行业包括文体娱（-17.0%） 、教育（-15.3%）和卫生社会（-14.0%） 。制造业行业分
类下，固投增速较高的细分行业包括铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业（15.8%） 、计算
机、通信和其他电子设备制造业（9.6%）和纺织业（4.2%），固投增速较低的行业包括食品制造业
（-10.3%）、金属制品业（-10.1%）和专用设备制造业（-8.9%）。 
与 1-8 月相比，一级分类行业中固投增速上行的行业是教育、制造业和公共事业，固投增速下行较
多的行业是文体娱、水利环境和农林牧渔业。制造业行业分类下，固投增速上行较多的行业是计算
机、通信和其他电子设备制造业、化学原料及化学制品制造业和电气机械及器材制造业，固投增速
下行较多的行业是纺织业、金属制品业和有色金属冶炼及压延加工业 。 
据统计局解读，1-9 月知识产权产品投资同比增长 9.3%，高技术产业投资同比增长 4.9%，其中信息
服务业，航空、航天器及设备制造业，电子及通信设备制造业投资分别增长 20.2%、12.5%、7.6%。

从居民收入来源来看，2026 年前三季度工资性收入同比增速 5.3%，经营净收入增速 6.6%，财产净
收入累计同比增速 1.6%，转移净收入增速 5.7%；从结构来看，工资性收入占比 57.4%，较 2026 年
上半年下降 0.5 个百分点，经营净收入占比上升 0.4 个百分点，财产净收入占比下降 0.2 个百分点，
转移净收入占比上升 0.3 个百分点。

2026 年前三季度居民的边际消费倾向较上半年上升。2026 年前三季度居民人均消费支出 22354 元，
同比增长 2.7%，扣除价格因素，实际增速增长 2.7%，与 2026 年上半年相比，居民消费增速持平。
2026 年前三季度，居民消费八大分类中，同比增速较高的是其他用品及服务、生活用品及服务、教
育文化娱乐和食品烟酒，与 2026 年上半年相比，增速上升超过 1.0 个百分点的仅有医疗保健；2026
年前三季度 居民消费支出结构中占比较高的 还是食品烟酒（ 29.7%）、居住（ 21.3%）和交通通信
（14.3%），与 2026 年上半年相比，占比上升超过 1.0 个百分点的是教育文化娱乐，与 2025 年同期
相比，占比上升的是食品烟酒、生活用品及服务、教育文化娱乐和其他用品及服务 ，占比下降的是
居住、交通通信和医疗保健。2026 年前三季度居民边际消费倾向较上半年小幅上升，但整体来看居
民消费意愿较弱，边际消费倾向和增量收入边际消费倾向对比 2025 年同期均有所下降， 特别是增量
收入边际消费倾向降幅较大。

### Page 12

2026 年 10 月 19 日 9 月和三季度经济数据点评 12

结论：内需出现向好迹象 
三季度经济增速超预期，全年稳增长压力下降 。三季度实际 GDP 同比增长 4.5%超出市场预期，带
动前三季度实际 GDP 累计同比增速达到 4.6%，略高于全年增速目标区间下限，加之去年四季度经
济增长基数偏低，一定程度上缓解了短期稳增长的压力。从 9 月经济数据看，稳增长的政策起到了
一定效果：工业增加值同比增速符合市场预期，同时维持在较高增速上；社零同比增速较 8 月明显
上升，表明内需仍有平稳释放的潜力；固定资产投资累计同比降幅持平于 1-8 月，但从我们计算的
当月增速看，基建投资和制造业投资增速均较 8 月出现较大幅度好转。从季节规律看，四季度经济
增长仍有支撑：一是生产端补库存，可能驱动工业增加值同比增速维持在当前的增速水平，考虑到
PPI 同比增速走高和 9 月出口交货值增速走高，价格因素和外需可能成为四季度生产端超预期的推
动力；二是四季度是传统消费旺季，从社零环比增速看，在 9 月环比高增长的基础上，四季度的三
个月在电商促销和春节前消费等因素推动下，环比仍能 维持较强的正增长；三是四季度也是海外消
费旺季，进出口或依赖我国生产端优势，持续受益于外需拉动。 
经济向“新”带动政策求“变”。 8 月底至今，从稳增长的角度出发，政策做了多方面调整：一是
国家发改委等部门召开全国投资工作推进会议，部署七项稳投资 工作，二是住建部等三部门推进商
品住房销售制度改革，稳定地产预期，三是证监会发文支持构建房地产发展新模式，四是央行调整
完善几项货币政策工具稳增长， 五是财政部安排使用地方政府债务结存限额5500 亿元， 六是中共中
央、国务院印发《关于发展新质生产力的意见》，继续大力支持经济转型升级等等。我们认为虽然
2026 年年内稳增长的压力减轻，但从中长期看，经济增长的挑战依然很大：从全球范围内看，包括
但不限于 AI 技术发展对生产关系的冲击、地缘局势变化对能源和通胀的影响、经济增长 K 型分化
在行业之间和国别之间带来的发展不均衡、各经济体面临的增长挑战与财政压力、发达国家货币政
策收缩与金融条件收紧对全球资本市场的外溢性影响等等；从国内看，我们面临的挑战依然是以维
持经济向新向优趋势和供强需弱的现实矛盾为主。 我们认为未来一段时间内，我国宏观经济的主要
表现或仍是增速缓步下台阶，因此积极的财政政策和适度宽松的货币政策发力重点或更多聚焦于托
底民生和实现社会公平，其次是发展先进生产力，然后才是稳增长。 尽管国内经济看起来只是“平
稳”，但我们认为相较于其他经济体，我国的比较优势依然非常明显 。

风险提示：全球通胀二次上行；欧美经济 超预期回落；国际局势复杂化。

评级体系说明 
以报告发布日后公司股价/行业指数涨跌幅相对同期相关市场指数的涨跌幅的表现为基准：  
公司投资评级： 
买    入：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 20%以上； 
增    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内超越基准指数 10%-20%； 
中    性：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数变动幅度在-10%-10%之间； 
减    持：预计该公司股价在未来 6-12 个月内相对基准指数跌幅在 10%以上； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

行业投资评级： 
强于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现强于基准指数； 
中    性：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现基本与基准指数持平； 
弱于大市：预计该行业指数在未来 6-12 个月内表现弱于基准指数； 
未有评级：因无法获取必要的资料或者其他原因，未能给出明确的投资评级。

沪深市场基准指数为沪深 300 指数；新三板市场基准指数为三板成指或三板做市指数；香港市场基准指数为恒生指数
或恒生中国企业指数；美股市场基准指数为纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数。

### Page 1

芯⽚指数⼋连跌，美科技七巨头指数单⽇下挫 1.6% ，本周累跌 2.87% 。存储概念指数当天
跌 6.28% 。存储芯⽚股 SK 海⼒⼠ ADR 跌超 6.5% ，闪迪跌超 6.8% ，美光科技跌超 5.6% 。光
通信公司 Coherent 跌逾 7% 。 10 年期美债收益率扭转三连降。美元指数创逾两周新低，离
岸⼈⺠币盘中再创四年新⾼。布油盘中涨超 3% ，美油七⽇连涨。
摘要：
科技股遭新⼀轮抛售， AI 投资盈利能⼒的疑虑与油价骤升形成双重压⼒，美股周五全线下挫。纳
指单⽇重挫 2% ，芯⽚股连续第⼋个交易⽇收跌，创下 2008 年以来最⻓连跌纪录。
周五，纳斯达克 100 指数收跌 2.5% ，标普 500 指数跌 1.1% 。云计算服务商 CoreWeave 单⽇暴跌
12% ，其债券收益率本周⼀度突破 15% ，信⽤⻛险攀升⾄通常预⽰财务困境的⽔平。
本周， AI 交易的核⼼⽭盾正从算⼒需求增⻓转向融资成本、资本开⽀回报和企业盈利能⼒。云服
务商 CoreWeave 债务承压，进⼀步引发市场对 AI 数据中⼼建设融资模式的审视；存储芯⽚、光

通信和数据中⼼设备等此前受益于 AI 投资热潮的股票，也成为资⾦减仓的重点。媒体指出，
CoreWeave 股价持续下跌令市场更关注 AI 交易的融资问题，未来⼏周科技巨头的财报将检验⾼
估值能否得到盈利⽀撑。
在能源层⾯，布伦特原油结算价收于每桶约 110 美元附近， WTI 原油单⽇涨幅达 3.8% ，报每桶
101.71 美元。美国两党议员联合提出法案，拟阻⽌特朗普政府与莫斯科达成的俄罗斯柴油⼊市
协议。
AI 盈利能⼒遭质疑，芯⽚股抛压持续
AI 基础设施投⼊能否产⽣⾜够回报，已成为市场新的核⼼争议。
周五美股开盘涨跌不⼀，随后卖压逐渐扩⼤。道指⾼开后早盘⼀度上涨超过 460 点、涨幅接近
0.9% ，但午盘转跌，跌幅⼀度接近 310 点、约 0.6% 。标普 500 指数微幅⾼开后转跌，午盘跌幅⼀
度接近 1.2% ；纳斯达克综合指数低开低⾛，收盘跌幅扩⼤⾄ 2.15% 。

其他指数中，纳斯达克 100 指数跌 2.5% ，⽣物科技指数⼀度跌超 2.5% ，费城半导体指数跌超
3.5% 。芯⽚股持续⾛弱，凸显市场对 AI 投资回报和融资模式的疑虑，已经从个别公司蔓延⾄整
个产业链。
AI 产业链股票成为抛售重灾区。存储概念指数当天跌 6.28% 。存储芯⽚股SK 海⼒⼠ ADR跌超

### Page 3

科技七巨头遭遇重挫。万得科技七巨头单⽇下挫 1.6% ，本周累跌 2.87% 。
嘉盛的 Fawad Razaqzada 表⽰：
CoreWeave 作为 AI 算⼒基础设施的早期⼊局者，⼤量依赖⾼收益债务市场为扩张融资，在当前
利率⾼企的背景下处境尤为脆弱。 Razaqzada 指出，相关⻛险已开始向信贷市场蔓延。 " 这对 AI
相关交易形成了两难困境 —— 利率⾛⾼既压低未来现⾦流的现值，同时⼜推⾼了⽀撑 AI 下⼀阶段
增⻓所需基础设施的融资成本。 "
据彭博社报道，以超⼤规模云服务商为代表的科技巨头年度资本⽀出正逼近 1 万亿美元，其中⼤
部分来⾃债务融资。彭博⾏业研究数据显⽰，尽管最⼤型科技公司多持有投资级信⽤评级，但
基⾦经理正对 CoreWeave 等垃圾级借款⽅提出越来越多的质疑。
并不是所有⼈都愿意跟着砍估值。⾼盛的 Rich Privorotsky 认为市场在局部反应过度：代币需求
仍在超预期增⻓，纳指的前瞻市盈率已跌到约 21 倍，为本轮 AI 周期启动以来最低。

美股资⾦轮动⾄防御与能源板块
本周的价格⾛势表明，市场的讨论重⼼正在转移。问题已不再是 AI 的市场空间有多⼤，⽽是实现
这⼀前景所需的巨额投⼊最终能否带来利润。

### Page 4

美股必需消费板块逆势收红，百事可乐、亿滋国际、好时同步⾛⾼，科技七巨头⾥苹果是唯⼀
收涨的⼀只，涨幅 0.43% 。
能源股是油价回升最直接的受益者。埃克森美孚当天预告炼油业务的利润将远超预期，柴油价
格处在历史⾼位正是原因，它与雪佛⻰的涨幅都排在标普 500 前列。
莫德纳是当天标普 500 ⾥跌幅最⼤的⼀只，盘中⼀度跌超 12% ，收盘跌 7.82% 。美国⻝品药品监
督管理局拒绝受理其季节性流感疫苗 mRNA-1010 的上市申请，给出的理由是三期临床试验缺少
“ 充分且严格对照 ” 的研究设计。
分化在周线层⾯看得更清楚。本周道指涨 0.68% ，纳指跌 4.48% ，两者周度表现差超过 6 个百分
点，为 2002 年 7 ⽉以来最⼤；当⽇恰逢⽉度期权到期， VIX 盘中⼀度升破 22 ，为四个⽉来⾸次。
沃什讲话未强化 10 ⽉加息预期
周五早盘， 9 ⽉进出⼝物价与⼯业产出数据公布后， 10 年期美债收益率⼀度上⾏ 3.8 个基点⾄
5.125% ；到收盘，这⼀涨幅收窄到不⾜ 1 个基点，收益率报 5.097% ，⽇内最⾼触及 5.133% 。

本周来看， 2 年期、 5 年期、 10 年期和 30 年期收益率分别下⾏ 3.8 个、 12.4 个、 13.4 个和 15.5 个基
点，⻓端明显跑赢短端，曲线⾛成⽜平形态。就⼀周⽽⾔，美国国债迎来了 2 ⽉以来最好的表
现。

### Page 5

沃尔什在曼⾕与国际货币基⾦组织总裁格奥尔基耶娃对话时拒绝表态，只说 “ 我不预判会议 ” ，并
称两次会议之间⼤体符合美联储的预期。投资者把它读成了 10 ⽉不会再动。
他还补充说，他不相信应该暗⽰ 2026 年剩余时间⾥他会采取什么⾏动，同时坚称美联储 “ 已经⾮
常明确地表⽰，我们将实现价格稳定 ” 。真正影响市场的是他评估道：
分析认为，对于⼀个在 9 ⽉份加息并预计今年还会加息⼀次的美联储来说，这番话听起来像是委
婉地暗⽰ 10 ⽉份会议上不会加息。
本周，尽管市场对 2026 年加息的预期基本持平（尽管存在波动），但对2027 年加息的预期却明
显下滑。
在两次会议之间，世界经济总体上符合我们的预期。

### Page 6

⾼盛⾸席经济学家 Jan Hatzius 团队写道，把沃尔什的表态与鲍曼的 “ 我没有紧迫感 ” 、戴利不愿
凭有限信息做决定放在⼀起看，联邦公开市场委员会将在 10 ⽉暂停，年内第⼆次加息留给 12
⽉。
⾼盛政府债券交易主管 Andrew Mullen 的说法更直⽩。市场多年来第⼀次真正⽆视⽕热的通胀数
据，避险买盘回来了， “ 如果⼀定要我说，这轮⾏情还有后劲 ” 。
美联储内部已经有⼈承认了这件事。理事沃勒本周早些时候表⽰，收益率上⾏已经在替美联储
分担⼀部分⼯作。也就是说紧缩不必完全靠加息完成，信⽤市场⾃⼰就能把⾦融条件收紧。
美元指数创逾两周新低，离岸⼈⺠币再创四年新⾼
汇市⽅⾯，美元指数周五⾛弱，势将结束此前连续四周上涨的⾛势。 ICE 美元指数（ DXY ）连续
两⽇盘中跌破 102.00 ，在周五欧股盘中跌⾄ 101.58 ，连续两⽇刷新 10 ⽉ 6 ⽇以来低位，⽇内跌近
0.5% 。
美元⾛弱推动⼈⺠币继续上⾏，离岸⼈⺠币（ CNH ）兑美元连续两⽇盘中涨破 6.69 ，美股早盘

曾逼近 6.6830 ，连续两⽇刷新 2022 年 7 ⽉以来盘中⾼位。
本周三和周四先后公布的美国 CPI 和 PPI 通胀数据⾼于预期，⼀度推动交易员增加对美联储进⼀
步加息的押注；但沃什周五的讲话没有强化 10 ⽉加息预期，叠加美债收益率此前回落，美元的
利率优势有所减弱。

### Page 7

欧元兑美元从近期低位回升，美元兑⽇元则在 157.9 附近波动。整体⽽⾔，市场正在重新权衡美
国通胀韧性、未来利率路径与 AI 投资⻛险。若科技股抛售进⼀步加剧，避险需求可能继续影响主
要货币⾛势，但美元能否恢复上涨仍取决于美国利率预期和全球⻛险偏好的变化。
布油盘中涨超 3% ，美油七连涨，⻩⾦企稳于 4100 美元上⽅
原油⽅⾯，国际油价周五盘中有所回落，但仍处⾼位。布伦特原油此前因中东供应⻛险⽽上
涨，周五盘中⼀度在 105 美元上⽅交易；美国 WTI 原油则在每桶 97 美元附近波动。
美股早盘传出美国两党众议员提出议案阻⽌特朗普的俄罗斯柴油松绑协议后，原油加速上⾏，
美股午盘之初，美国 WTI 原油曾接近 100.90 美元，⽇内涨近 3% ，布伦特原油曾逼近 109.50 美
元，⽇内涨逾 3.5% 。
市场继续关注霍尔⽊兹海峡周边紧张局势，以及相关事件对全球原油运输和供应的影响。
油价维持⾼位也令宏观市场难以完全摆脱通胀担忧。能源成本上升可能推⾼整体通胀，并使美
联储更难迅速转向宽松；但在周五的交易中，投资者对 AI 产业链融资⻛险的关注，同样主导了股

票市场的⽅向。
到周五纽约时段尾盘，现货⻩⾦报 4,168.69 美元，全天跌 0.05% ，⼏乎收平。 12 ⽉加息选项仍然
留在桌⾯上，这是压制它反弹空间的主要原因。现货⽩银报 56.85 美元，跌 0.70% 。

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Stronger Growth, Costlier Money
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
The latest GDP report and annual revisions show a sturdier U.S. economy than previously
reported. According to the Bureau of Economic Analysis (BEA), real GDP grew at a 2.2%
annualized pace in Q2 compared to 1.5% previously estimated, and Q1 was revised
from 2.1% to 2.5%. Both 2024 and 2025 were also revised slightly higher. Most of the

first half of the year revision was in categories related to the Artificial Intelligence (AI)
buildout, but real consumer spending has also been revised slightly higher, accelerating
from 0.7% annualized growth in Q1 to 3.8% in Q2 despite surging energy prices and
slow hiring.
Spending in July and August remained much stronger than labor market conditions alone
would suggest, also averaging 3.7% annualized growth. Upside revisions to net interest

income and personal saving suggest households have benefited from more income than
was previously apparent, with personal saving revised higher back to 2023. The new data,
according to the BEA, show the January to July 2026 savings rate averaged 4.8% versus
3.3% prerevision, strengthening consumer spending underpinnings despite moderate
hiring, high energy prices and rising borrowing costs.
The BEA update also included inflation methodological changes related to categories

inflated by the AI boom—such as portfolio management fees and computer software and
accessories. These improved the inflation picture to some extent—August “core” PCE
inflation was reduced by 0.3 ppts to 3% YoY, bringing it closer to “core” consumer price
index inflation, which was 2.5% YoY and 2% on a three-month annualized basis. While still
above the 2% Federal Reserve (Fed) target, “core” PCE now appears stable at 3% year over
year since January rather than accelerating. These methodological changes are expected

to dampen pass-through of AI-related price pressures into “core” inflation and effects of
stock-market swings on inflation.
They also produced an encouraging improvement in recent PCE inflation momentum.
Three-month annualized “core” PCE inflation slowed from 4.3% at the peak of tariff
effects in February to 2.1% in August. For durable goods, this rate was down from 5.8%
in February to 1.5% in August, and, for services ex-energy and healthcare, it slowed from

4.2% to 2.2%, for example. Inflation is volatile on a three-month change basis, so it
remains to be seen whether this moderation continues without further Fed tightening.
For now, the trends in the San Francisco Fed’s cyclical versus acyclical inflation measures
diverged again in August. As expected, given softening wage growth, prices most sensitive
to the labor market have eased further. At 3% YoY, this inflation category is down from
8% in early 2023 and 4.6% two years ago is back to prepandemic levels and is likely

to ease more (Exhibit 1A). In contrast, supply bottlenecks, high energy prices and other
industry-specific factors have kept acyclical inflation on an uptrend. As shown in Exhibit
1B, the relentless increase in freight costs could prolong upside pressure on this category.
The diesel market has tightened in recent months, boosting its price by 70% YoY,
consistent with Brent oil at around $200 per barrel and large reverberations across
a wide swath of goods and services prices. A government-policy-related decline in

available truck drivers is also raising transportation costs more than expected. So are
tight ocean shipping capacity and longer/more expensive voyages due to the U.S./Iran
War. Supply-chain stress also increased as companies rushed to front-run potential new
tariff increases.
On the positive side for the inflation outlook, unit labor costs (ULC) are up modestly
as robust productivity growth has absorbed much of the wage growth, limiting a major
source of domestic inflation. Upside productivity growth revisions are likely to further

reduce ULC, which could help shift focus from energy and AI-related inflation to
eventual disinflationary effects once oil and refined fuel prices start to ease. Sustained
productivity gains would support growth and margins while limiting inflation and the
need for much higher interest rates.
Investment Implications
We favor selective Equity exposure
to companies with durable demand
and pricing power, while remaining
cautious about businesses most

exposed to fuel, freight, and
financing costs. We would also
gradually increase long-duration
exposure.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
Surging nominal growth has boosted corporate revenues (up 13% YoY in Q2, according to the
BEA) while contained unit labor costs helped raise margins and profits more than expected.
With labor costs declining as a share of revenues, investment-related tax incentives and
rock-bottom net interest expense, pre-tax nonfinancial corporate profits as measured in GDP
accounts rose 21% YoY in Q2. Total corporate profits after-tax were up 18.2% YoY, according to

the BEA.
Surging AI-related imports and investment are keeping the business and profits cycle on
an uptrend both here and abroad. According to BofA Global Research, the global economic
cycle backdrop improved further in September, as reflected in a broad-based increase in its
Global Wave1 indicator. Broadening U.S. manufacturing strength, fueled by ongoing AI-driven
investment, a healthier orders backlog and low inventories suggest that the expansion has

room to run—a constructive environment for earnings. Typically, a Global Wave upswing is
consistent with rising earnings revisions ratios and equity price uptrends. According to the
Global Wave, global earnings growth expectations remain exceptionally strong.
Still, absent a resolution of war-related energy and transportation disruptions, the same
industrial strength intensifies inflation pressures. A prolonged supply shock raises the risk of
restrictive interest rates and weakening economic growth. In this context, the surge in the

10-year Treasury yield above 5% is starting to reflect more than optimism about U.S. growth
and strengthening productivity outlook. Oil’s tightest recent correlation with 10-year Treasury
yields in 35 years suggests a common energy-price thread behind rising yields in developed
countries.
Bond market spillovers from normalizing Japan yields and capital repatriation are another
common thread. Higher yields on foreign sovereign bonds such as in Japan—which has

escaped stagnation and deflation by intensely pursuing investment-led growth—weaken the
relative appeal of Treasurys. That distinction matters for portfolios. A yield increase driven
mainly by durable productivity gains would come with a stronger outlook for future income
and earnings. A yield increase driven by cost shocks and greater uncertainty raises financing
costs without the same benefit.
In sum, the economy remains more resilient than expected, but the bond market is also

charging more for an uncertain inflation outlook given untethered government debt and
cooling global demand for Treasurys spurred by geopolitical tensions and Japan capital
repatriation. The case for much further tightening becomes stronger if wage growth
strengthens, productivity lags, and unit labor costs reaccelerate, causing cyclical inflation to
also pick up steam. The case becomes weaker if that measure stays contained while fuel and
freight pressures recede or rising Treasury yields reduce asset prices and economic growth.

spike feed acyclical inflation.

4 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
Yields, Yen and Iran: What Haunts the “House”
Lauren Sanfilippo, Director and Senior Investment Strategist
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
We recently highlighted the three-front challenge facing Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent:
engineering a stronger yen, containing the 10-year Treasury yield and securing a diplomatic
resolution to the conflict with Iran.

Bessent recently has delivered one of the year’s more memorable lines while discussing
Treasury policy and the yen: “I have asymmetric information. I am the house now.” 2 Yet several
challenges remain: the 10-year Treasury yield is near levels last seen in the early 2000s, the
yen remains structurally weaker, and a phased agreement to reopen the Strait of Hormuz
appears remote. The resulting strain on energy markets keeps inflation risks elevated.

Put differently, oil prices, Treasury yields and the dollar-yen exchange rate are all trending
against Bessent’s objectives. Below, we examine how each variable is haunting his “house.”
Haunting the “House” is the Yen. Since the U.S. and Japan mounted a record $98.7B
coordinated intervention in late July after the yen weakened to a 40-year low near 164 per
dollar, the currency has appreciated roughly 4% against the dollar. The move was further
supported on September 18 when the BoJ raised rates by 25 basis points (bps) to 1.25%,

their highest level since the mid-1990s (Exhibit 2A). However, the split 7–2 vote and Governor
Ueda’s limited forward guidance leave the path of further tightening uncertain. The key
question is whether additional BoJ hikes can produce sustained appreciation in the yen, or
merely reinforce the temporary relief provided by the summer intervention.
Bessent, meanwhile, has repeatedly signaled his preference for a stronger yen. Partly because
yen stability is also a U.S. financing concern. Persistent currency weakness could prompt

Japan to support its currency by selling foreign assets, including U.S. Treasurys—which would
be an unwelcome prospect as Washington partially relies on foreign capital flows to fund its
large fiscal deficits. That potential “haunts” the Treasury Secretary.  At precisely the moment
Washington needs foreign capital to finance its enormous fiscal deficits, it would be inopportune
for one of America’s largest creditors to become a significant seller of Treasury securities.

Exhibit 2: Charting the “Yentervention” and U.S. Treasury Yields Surge Across the Curve.
A) The Yen Regains Some Strength. B) 5-Year Forward Yields Hold Above 5%.

Haunting the “House” are Yields. The surge in yields runs counter to a White House intent
on lowering borrowing costs. The benchmark 10-year yield has broken above the 5% threshold
and climbed to levels not seen since 2007. At around 5.30%, it stands more than a full
percentage point above where it began the year (Exhibit 2B). The level is striking but so is the
speed of the move: Stronger-than-expected economic data, including a survey showing U.S.

2 September 8, 2026.
Investment Implications
While some key variables are
complicating the macro backdrop
(i.e., higher-for-longer yields/oil)
the combination of strong earnings
growth, robust consumer demand
(real final sales to private domestic
purchasers grew at 4.6% for Q2) and
ongoing capital investment suggests
that the fundamental backdrop for
U.S. Equities remains constructive.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
business activity expanded at the fastest pace in more than five years, helped trigger an 18
bps surge in the 10-year yield—among its largest daily moves in decades. The 5-year yield also
topped 5% for the first time since 2007, while the 30-year yield reached 5.63%, its highest
level since 2002.
Compounding the move is inflation, running above the Fed’s 2% target for 67 months,

large fiscal deficits and the prospect of continued growth in Treasury issuance. Against this
backdrop, Treasury bought back $4.1 billion of long-dated debt, versus a maximum of $6
billion. That effort has done little to arrest the selloff. A subsequent auction priced nearly $4
billion of 7-year notes at 5.085%, the highest yield for that maturity in 33 years.
All in, the MOVE index, a key gauge of Treasury market volatility, jumped from 78 to 95 on

Wednesday September 23 and then to 104 the following day, its highest level since the early
days of the Iran war (Exhibit 3A).
Haunting the “House” is the Iran conflict. Early expectations that military pressure and
economic sanctions would quickly bring Iran to the negotiating table have yet to materialize.
Iranian officials have signaled that a full reopening of the Strait of Hormuz remains unlikely
while sanctions stay in place. Although talks between Washington and Tehran are continuing,

conflicting signals and lingering skepticism from both sides have kept tensions elevated.
As a result, oil prices have remained elevated in a range even as some Middle East supply
disruptions began to ease.
Startling is that Brent crude is up more than 60% this year, but refined product prices
have risen even faster. Ongoing disruptions to energy flows through the Strait of Hormuz,
combined with Ukrainian strikes on Russian refining infrastructure have tightened global

supplies of crude oil and refined fuels. Saudi Arabia has restored some drone-disrupted flows,
providing roughly 3.5 million barrels a day of relief (roughly 3% of global consumption).
The squeeze is mostly acute in diesel markets, where inventories remain constrained and
prices continue to climb (Exhibit 3B). U.S. diesel prices recently surpassed $6.50 per gallon,
the highest level on record, just as Russia has extended restrictions on diesel exports into

October. Against this backdrop, proposals to ban U.S. fuel exports risks worsening the
imbalance. According to S&P Global, an export ban could reduce refinery runs by nearly 2
million barrels per day and significantly cut gasoline production, potentially driving gasoline
and jet fuel prices even higher. As long as tensions persist, the oil impulse remains a key
impediment to structurally lower inflation.
Investment implications. Treasury Secretary Bessent increasingly sits at the intersection of

monetary policy, fiscal policy, foreign policy and national security. We remain constructive on
the U.S. economy and overweight U.S. Equities but are watching as these fronts challenge the
narrative. There is a great deal riding on the “house” versus the key macro variables of today.

6 of 8 October 5, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THOUGHT OF THE WEEK
When It Comes to Attracting Foreign Capital, the U.S. Remains Exceptional
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
There may be no better vote of confidence in a country than the willingness of
foreigners to put their money there. And by that measurement, the U.S. remains the
exception—both portfolio and FDI inflows are currently running at record highs based

on the latest data. This dynamic is one key reason why we remain constructive on U.S.
assets over the longer term.
Notwithstanding all the handwringing about America’s fiscal deficits, tariffs and rising
government debt, global investors sunk some $942B in net purchases of U.S. Equities in
the 12 months to July, the highest rolling 12-month total on record. In Q2, net purchase
of U.S. Equities soared to $426B, up 62% from the same period a year ago (Exhibit 4A).

By country, flows were down from China—not surprisingly—but strong from Taiwan and
South Korea, who have taken to recycling their massive AI-generated trade surplus back
into the U.S. In the end, strong portfolio inflows suggest that international investors
remain willing to increase their exposure to the world's largest capital market—that’s
bullish for U.S. assets.
Meanwhile, Exhibit 4B highlights the growing push among foreign multinationals to be

inside the U.S., with FDI inflows totaling nearly $240B in the first six months of this year.
That is a staggering 103% jump from a year ago and means FDI inflows are on track for
a record year, estimated at $480B for 2026.
There are multiple factors behind surging inflows: One, due to rising U.S. trade
protectionism, foreign multinationals increasingly want to be “inside” the U.S. economy,
not outside. Two, within the $32 trillion-plus economy with extraordinarily consumer

purchasing power, foreign firms prefer to be closer to their customers via investment
than just trade (exports). Three, America’s massive investment cycle in AI, data centers,
semiconductors, power generation, etc., has created numerous potential investment
opportunities for many foreign firms that don’t want to miss out on America’s surging
capex cycle. Four, and finally, abundant energy supplies, deep capital markets and a highly
developed technological ecosystem have also induced more FDI inflows.

In the end, double-barreled capital inflows speak to the absolute and relative
attractiveness of the U.S. economy and capital markets. Stay invested in America—the
rest of the world is.
Exhibit 4: U.S. Exceptionalism: Record Inflows of Portfolio and FDI Inflows.
A) Net Foreign Purchases of US Equities and Investment Funds. B) FDI Inflows to the U.S.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

ICE BofA MOVE Index is a market-implied measure of U.S. bond market volatility.
Consumer Price Index measures the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a market basket of consumer goods and services.
Baltic Dry Index is a daily shipping and trade index published by the Baltic Exchange in London that measures the cost of moving raw materials like iron ore, coal, and grain by sea.
Volatility Index (VIX) is a real-time market indicator that measures the stock market's expected volatility over the next 30 days.
